- Suppliers often have the strongest sellers in their range and can name them when asked. This helps set a bestseller list before any sales data exists. Asking which products sell best is a quick way to decide which products get the larger ad budget. *Older advice (2024).* [L0360.011]

### Price and Margin Filters

- When a supplier provides both MSRP and MAP, check that MAP is profitable. Show MSRP as the strike-through compare-at price and sell at MAP, which is only a minimum, so a higher price is also allowed. *Older advice (2020).* [L1088.021]
- Set the niche's price filter in the product selection. Sources give different thresholds, which apply to different stores:
  - Products priced above $400 from a supplier's full catalog are acceptable to list. Suppliers do not require you to list everything they offer. *Older advice (2025 call).* [L0278.027]
  - On a 2024 call, the example was to list only the 10 products over $200, or only the 50 massage chairs from a 1,000-product catalog. [L0805.020]
  - The 2023 rule is to upload products priced above the niche average and with a MAP at least 2X wholesale. [L0251.037]
- Before listing a supplier's product, check whether other sellers mark it up at all. If other sellers mark it up only slightly, for example around 10 percent, drop that supplier. If margins are strong, go ahead. *Older advice (2021).* [L0801.036]

### Bundles, Add-ons and Pairs

- Source physical product bonuses from the same supplier as the main product whenever possible. Both items then ship together in one order, which removes extra fulfillment work and reduces coordination. *Older advice (no year given).* [L0314.003]
- Add-on products should come from the same brand as the main product where possible. This simplifies shipping and keeps margins higher. Look in the same supplier's catalog first, and search other suppliers only when no related product exists. *Older advice (2023).* [L0964.031]
- If suppliers and products are sold as a pair or set, upload them as sold, with photos showing the pair or set. *Older advice (2021).* [L0671.170]
- Procedure for a bundle offer, *Source C*:
  1. Agree with the supplier on a dedicated SKU for the bundle offer.
  2. Make sure the supplier ships all items of that bundle in one package.
  3. Add that specific SKU to the store so every bundle order triggers the single-package shipment.
  4. Check the bundle's cost of goods and shipping cost before running ads.

  [L0502.019]
- Market labels in bundle rows: do not use a cost or price row labelled for another market, such as UK, for USA pricing. Change the market label to US before entering any values in a bundle row. [L1182.013]

### Product Identifiers: GTIN, UPC, MPN and SKU

#### Which identifier to use

- Use the supplier's UPC as the product identifier when it exists, since it lets Shopify and Google Merchant Center match your listing against identical products. Use the MPN when there is no UPC. *Older advice (2023).* [L0934.155]
- Use the supplier's SKU only when the supplier has no GTIN (a product identifier that competitors and Google use to index products). If the supplier has a GTIN, use the GTIN. *Older advice (2023).* [L0835.037]
- Do not create a made-up SKU to fill in the product identifier field. SKUs are retailer-specific and not universal, so use the supplier's UPC or MPN instead. *Older advice (2023).* [L0934.156]
- Use the identifier the supplier actually has, and do not invent codes. A supplier without GTIN codes can be used with its MPN or SKU as the identifier. *Older advice (2023).* [L0855.065]
- Google Merchant Center requires a GTIN for listings. If a supplier provides none, use the manufacturer part number (MPN) in that field and generate your own internal identifier for these products. *Older advice (2021).* [L1279.037]
- If a product has no GTIN and Google Merchant Center reports an error for it, use the MPN, as Google's own guidance describes. An MPN does not hurt Google Shopping when no GTIN exists. *Older advice (2023).* [L0835.039]
- Enter the supplier's model or part number as the SKU, and never invent your own. The SKU appears on the internal order sheet, and the supplier needs that exact number to fulfil the order. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1166.015]
- Only ask suppliers for barcode numbers (UPC, GTIN or EAN) when Merchant Center product diagnostics show a barcode problem, not for every product upfront. Products with no barcode from the manufacturer do not need one and should not be suspended for that. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0727.029]

#### Sources disagree on the supplier SKU

- Older advice (2020) says to use the supplier's SKU number as the MPN, and to create an internal identifier for products with no SKU, GTIN or UPC (L0664.021).
- Older advice (2020) says that when a SKU is missing, you create an internal identifier from the MPN and record it consistently (L1111.045).
- The 2023 advice orders the identifiers: UPC first, then MPN when no UPC exists, and the supplier SKU only when no GTIN exists (L0934.155, L0835.037).
- The 2021 advice says to use the MPN in the GTIN field when no GTIN exists, and to generate an internal identifier for these products (L1279.037).
- The newest advice (2023) is the most specific. Use it first, and use the older rules only for stores set up under them. [L0664.021, L1111.045, L0934.155, L0835.037, L1279.037]

#### Finding a missing GTIN or UPC

- Procedure to find a GTIN or UPC that the supplier does not provide, *older advice (2024)*:
  1. Search the supplier's product name or brand name plus product name on Google Shopping.
  2. Open the product pages of the stores that appear and look for a UPC, part number or SKU in the description.
  3. Search that code on Google and check whether other stores list the same product under it.
  4. If other stores confirm the code, use it.
  5. If no code can be found at all, use the MPN instead, following Google's documentation on MPN.

  [L0965.035]
- Procedure to identify a product by the supplier's SAKU-style SKU, *older advice (2023)*:
  1. Take the SKU the supplier gives you, which may be its GTIN, and search for it on Google.
  2. Check the results to see which competitors sell from the same supplier.
  3. If competitors use that number as the product identifier, use it too.
  4. If there is no GTIN, use the SKU as the product identifier. It will not affect Google Ads.

  [L0835.038]

### Images, Descriptions and Content Rights

#### Get permission before using supplier content

- Use a supplier's product photos on your store only after the supplier has confirmed in writing, even when the photos come from the supplier's own website. Asking first avoids disputes. *Older advice (2025).* [L0972.012]
- You may use product images from a supplier's website after authorization. Email the supplier contact to confirm this is acceptable before uploading images to your store. *Older advice (2024).* [L0276.028]
- Before using a supplier's product descriptions or lifestyle photos, ask which content you may use. If unsure, email for a yes or no on each part. *Older advice (2023).* [L0934.044]
- Ask suppliers which product photos and descriptions you may use before copying them. Get a simple yes or no on each part of their content. *Older advice (2023).* [L0934.150]

Template for that request (*older, 2025*):

```
Hi [supplier contact name],

We are close to sending traffic to our product pages, and Google Shopping requires a main product photo on a white background for each product. We can see photos on your website that would work for this. Could you confirm in writing that we may use them for [product names]? If you have a white-background photo of each product, sending those would also be very helpful.

Thank you,
[your name]
[your store name]
```
[L0972.097]

- Do not use photos taken from other sellers. Ask the supplier for product photos or for the rights to use its images, and check its website, social pages and events for material. *Older advice (2018).* [L0466.040]
- Do not use product photos from Alibaba or AliExpress unless the supplier has given permission for them. Photos from non-approved sources should not be used. *Older advice (2022).* [L1113.030]
- Product photos and videos on AliExpress or Alibaba listings can be used for ads only after confirming with the supplier that the content is its own. Some sellers copy custom content from others, which creates copyright risk. [L0693.018]
- Product photos taken from AliExpress are hard to use on their own. A seller who shows the product in its own real setting and photos has a stronger store. [L0530.014]
- A product that is new on AliExpress with few sellers has few competitor listings to study. Product images may then need to come from the supplier listing. [L1194.049]
- Ask a supplier for high-resolution product images through the sales rep or contact first, and also ask for older images, such as from trade shows. Do not take images from other websites. [L0359.034]
- Supplier spec sheets, lifestyle images or videos can replace the supplier's copy. When a supplier tells you to build product pages by copying its own website content, its operations are probably weak, but this is not a dealbreaker. Ask the sales rep for other content sources such as spec sheets, lifestyle images or videos, and use them to build product pages that go beyond the supplier's copy. *Older advice (2020).* [L0358.001]
- Installation guides, PDF manuals or videos from the supplier can be added to the product page as download links or embeds. Do not create such manuals yourself. *Older advice (2020).* [L0664.020]

#### Photo sourcing

- Do not take your own product photos. Use supplier or stock images, except for rare trade show photos. *Older advice (2022).* [L0969.099]
- For resellers and dropshippers without the physical product, photographing products at manufacturer or dealer showrooms creates unique images that competitors relying on stock photography or generic supplier images will not have. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0301.033]
- Sources differ on taking your own photos. The 2022 rule allows only rare trade show photos (L0969.099). The 2021-22 example recommends showroom photos for resellers without the product (L0301.033). Use the 2022 rule as the baseline, and treat the showroom example as older.
- Once suppliers are connected, upload their product photos to Canva and crop them to the same size as the other store images. Uniform sizes keep the homepage collection tiles and banners looking consistent. *Older advice (no year given).* [L0342.043]
- When supplier images are unlabeled, identify the products in this order, *older advice (2025)*:
  1. Ask the supplier for a set of images where file names or SKU numbers match the products.
  2. If none exist, go to google.com, click the Search by image icon (Google Lens), upload the supplier photo, and click search.
  3. Use the matching product pages to identify the product. Do not copy images found on other sites because of copyright.

  [L0972.008]
- Steps to gather product images when the supplier sends too few or poor ones:
  1. After approval, request the product images together with the price list from the supplier.
  2. If the supplier sends only one photo, bad photos, or none, search Google Image Search by product name or SKU and collect every high-quality image you find.
  3. Ask your supplier contact whether it has attended trade shows and, if so, request photos of its best-selling products set up there.
  4. Upload all images as additional product images and mix them with the supplier's stock photos.

  [L1130.015]
- Trade show photos show products set up and displayed as a real range, which makes the offer look bigger and more professional. Mixing them with stock photos makes the product page stand out. [L1130.016]

### Reviews and Warranties

- Suppliers want you to make sales, so ask before displaying their product reviews on your site. Most suppliers agree, but never display reviews without asking first. *Older advice (2020).* [L0551.019]
- Ask suppliers for existing reviews and use them on the store only with the brand's explicit permission. These reviews come from the supplier, not from other retailers. *Older advice (2025).* [L0667.062]
- Procedure to get reviews for new product pages, *older advice (2020)*:
  1. Ask the brand's sales rep for reviews of the brand and its products.
  2. Check the supplier's own website for reviews first.
  3. If reviews exist, ask permission to use them.
  4. If none exist, ask whether the supplier has any reviews to share.

  [L0967.029]
- Brand names should be included on product pages, because brand names are search terms that drive traffic to the store. *Older advice (2020).* [L0967.030]
- A supplier can approve you without the store name appearing on pages. Naming brands on product pages helps the store rank for the brand names it sells. *Older advice (2020).* [L0967.031]
- Reviews from a supplier's brand may be used on your store only with the supplier's explicit permission. If the supplier agrees, the reviews can be used on your site, or on its Trustpilot page where they are published. If the supplier has no reviews or declines, rely on reviews from your own customers. [L1245.009]
- Procedure for using supplier reviews:
  1. Contact the brands you are already authorized to sell and ask whether they have product reviews you can use on your website.
  2. Continue only if the supplier gives explicit permission to use the reviews. Without it, do not use them.
  3. If permission is given, open the product page on your store as a customer would.
  4. Enter the supplier's review there, the same way a customer would enter a review.
  5. The review is published to the product page after submission.

  [L1245.010]
- Using reviews copied from a supplier's brand or website without permission is a mistake. Permission must be confirmed before any supplier review is placed on your store. [L1245.011]
- Show a supplier's warranty on the product pages of products from suppliers that offer one. *Older advice (2020).* [L1088.014]

### Sizing for Apparel and Footwear

#### Why sizes go wrong

- Chinese sizes are usually smaller than US and European sizes, so a size that fits a Chinese buyer may not fit a US or European buyer. Check the supplier's size charts against the target market before listing clothing or shoes. [L1128.013]
- Putting the size chart in the product description did not stop buyers from ordering the wrong size, even when the text was highlighted and buyers were told to order larger. Make size selection easier with a clear size chart, and expect fit problems with winter clothing and jackets. [L1128.016]
- For fashion products with sizing, verify the size chart with the supplier before selling. Size charts on competitor stores were often weak, so a better chart is an advantage. [L1161.013]
- Trusting the supplier's size labels for shoes leads to wrong-size orders, refunds and complaints. The manual conversion work is slow at the start but is necessary. [L1219.004]

#### Checks before listing

- Check after the ad validation step, before testing:
  1. Source the product and confirm a good price from the supplier.
  2. For fashion items, confirm correct sizing and a reliable size chart with the supplier, such as CJ or another dropshipping supplier.
  3. Only then start the test.

  [L1161.014]
- For fashion products, CJ and Best Dealer are examples of suppliers from which to get a verified size chart before listing. Use the chart from the supplier that actually fulfills the order, since sizing differs between suppliers. [L1161.051]
- Footwear sizing check before listing, *Source C*:
  1. Ask the supplier to send the length in centimeters for each size.
  2. Compare each centimeter length with the size standard of the target market, for example EU sizes for Europe.
  3. If the supplier advertises a size that does not match its centimeters, sell it under the size that matches the measurement. For example, sell an EU 34 measurement as 34 rather than 36.
  4. Repeat this check for each target market.

  [L0661.014]
- For footwear, ask the supplier for the real length of each size in centimeters, because supplier size labels do not match EU, UK or US sizing. Convert those centimeter values yourself for the target market instead of trusting the labels. [L1219.001]
- Chinese shoe size labels usually run one to two sizes smaller than European sizes. The lesson's example is Chinese 34 corresponding to about EU 32. Convert sizes manually before you publish a size chart. [L1219.002]
- Build a shoe size guide in this order:
  1. Get the centimeter length of every size from the supplier.
  2. Check in the target country which shoe size matches that length.
  3. Adjust the size numbers manually for that market.
  4. Publish the resulting size guide on the product page.

  [L1219.003]
- Procedure to avoid size chargebacks when selling shoes across markets:
  1. Ask the supplier for its size chart with the exact length in centimeters for each size, for example size 6.
  2. Ask for the European, Australian (or other local market) and American size charts.
  3. Compare each size with the local market chart and adjust the sizes you list.
  4. Double-check before publishing, because the same size number can differ between suppliers and markets.

  [L1214.011]

### Demo Products and Upload Sequencing

#### Demo products

- Start a new store with a handful of demo products, then remove them once the first supplier approval arrives. Continue working through the supplier list for more approvals. *Older advice (2020).* [L1111.044]
- Upload demo products to the store while waiting for supplier approval, then replace them with real brand products once approved. Do not leave products from low-quality suppliers on the store. *Older advice (2024).* [L0806.031]
- Remove demo products from the store as soon as the first live supplier products are uploaded. *Older advice (2020).* [L1088.025]
- Remove all demo products in Shopify after the first supplier approval, before uploading that supplier's products. *Older advice (2023).* [L0934.043]
- Upload only real, live products from approved suppliers, and remove demo products before turning on ads. Get at least a second supplier approval before running any traffic. *Older advice (2023).* [L1094.013]
- Upload only real products from approved suppliers and remove demo products before turning on ads. Secure a second supplier approval before buying any traffic. Do not open paid ad accounts until live products are on the store, because doing so early can be read as a red flag by Google. *Older advice (2023).* [L1094.118]
- Find 4 to 5 demo products from a store in another country, using a VPN, and upload them into the Shopify account the same way as shown before moving on to the next step. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0452.023]
- Real supplier products will later be uploaded with the same method once suppliers have been contacted and their products are available, so this process is useful later as well. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0452.024]

#### Upload timing

- Upload a supplier's products to the store immediately after approval and product information arrive, not in a batch later. Each delay between approval and upload leaves the store without products to sell. *Older advice (no year given).* [L0495.003]
- Start uploading products as soon as the first supplier is approved and has sent product data, without waiting for all supplier contacts to be made. Slow or unresponsive suppliers should not delay the upload work. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1163.001]
- While waiting for approval from preferred suppliers, immediately upload products from authorized non-MAP suppliers to your store, even over a weekend. Non-MAP suppliers typically approve faster than MAP-enforcing brands. Although you will not run paid ads to non-MAP products, their presence significantly boosts store credibility. When a decision maker from a preferred supplier visits your site, they see 3+ brands with hundreds of products, which dramatically increases your odds of approval on subsequent outreach. Pricing non-MAP items for profit margins without ad spend ensures you capture revenue if customers buy organically while protecting cash flow if they don't. [L0294.004]
- Keep a supplier's product pages live at launch only after approval. Before approval, a store may be shown unlocked without active purchase capability, and products are added only once real suppliers are approved. *Older advice (2021).* [L0834.022]
- Product uploads sequenced by the first-approval rule, *older advice (2023)*:
  1. Get approved by the first supplier.
  2. Remove all demo products in Shopify.
  3. Upload all products from that supplier.
  4. Contact the remaining suppliers on your list.

  [L0934.043]
- Sources differ on when to contact more suppliers. The 2023 advice says to contact the remaining suppliers after the first approval (L0934.043). The 2025 advice says to upload the first three approved suppliers' products and remove demo products before contacting more (L0966.022). Use the 2025 rule when you can reach three approvals, and the 2023 rule as the minimum.
- Get at least five approved suppliers before the module is complete. This creates an ongoing cycle of supplier outreach, approval, product integration, and store scaling. *Older advice (no year given).* [L0128.013]
- Upload the top products first at launch, *older advice (2021)*:
  1. For each approved supplier, open the top three competitor stores in tabs.
  2. Search each brand name there and sort by best sellers.
  3. Upload the best sellers first at launch.
  4. Upload the remaining catalog over time.

  [L0834.023]
- Choose which products from each approved supplier to upload first, *older advice (2024)*:
  1. Find the top future competitors' stores.
  2. Sort their product lists by best sellers (or featured or recommended).
  3. Upload the top 20 percent of best sellers from each approved supplier first.
  4. Upload the remaining 80 percent in the background over time.
  5. Optionally, ask each supplier rep for their current best-selling SKUs.

  [L0856.062]
- Sources differ on the first upload: the 2021 procedure uploads the best sellers at launch and the rest over time, and the 2024 procedure uploads the top 20 percent first and the other 80 percent in the background. Both apply the same logic; use the 2024 split if you need a fixed ratio.

## Shopify Setup

### Store structure

- Check whether each Shopify store uses a separate Shopify Payments account. Separate accounts make it easier to track each store's payments. *Older advice (2020).* [L0852.112]
- On Shopify, you can list 1,000 products on day one without restrictions. [L0280.009]

### Product fields

- Set the Weight field in the Shopify Shipping section, with a unit selector such as kg. It is used to calculate shipping rates at checkout and to price shipping labels during fulfillment. Demo products can leave it empty, but real supplier products need it filled in correctly. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0452.026]
- Enter stock levels on each product page when the supplier provides them. If the supplier gives no stock data, mark the product as in stock with a placeholder quantity, and confirm availability with the supplier. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1145.034]
- If no stock figure is available, enter 10 as a placeholder in Track quantity. A quantity of 0 shows the product as out of stock and blocks sales. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1166.016]
- Sources differ on the placeholder stock value. Both rules use a placeholder. The 2021-22 Source B advice names 10 as the Track quantity value (L1166.016), and the same course's note on the Stock field advises a placeholder quantity (L1145.034). Use 10 when the setting is Track quantity.
- Tick "This is a physical product" for physical goods. Weight and customs fields can be left empty when shipping domestically only. Customs details matter only for cross-border shipping: the country of origin, usually where the product is made, and the HS code used by border officers. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1166.017]
- Mark shipped items as physical products and enter their weight. For cross-border sales, add customs information on the product page. *Source D 2026.* [L1167.024]

### Microsoft Merchant Center feed via the Shopify app

- Submit a Shopify product feed to Microsoft Merchant Center through the feed app 'Feed for Google Shopping', in this order:
  1. In Shopify admin, open the app 'Feed for Google Shopping' and go to the header menu item 'Integrations'.
  2. Under 'Bing Shopping', click the green 'Connect' button (if not yet connected).
  3. Click 'Continue with integration'.
  4. Select the Microsoft Advertising account to use.
  5. Select the Microsoft Merchant Center store to use.
  6. Click 'Confirm your account' and check that the status shows connected and turned on.
  7. In Microsoft Advertising, go to Feeds and refresh the page. Two feeds should appear: the empty default catalog and the new feed named like 'US Bing Shopping Feed by <app name>'.
  8. Wait for the products to be imported. They show as 'pending' in Merchant Center when set up correctly.

  [L1036.001]
- A default catalog is created automatically when a new store is set up. The feed app's automatic feed is named 'US Bing Shopping Feed by <app name>' and is the feed that carries the Shopify products. The default catalog stays empty and can be ignored for this setup. [L1036.002]
- In Feeds, the button 'Create feed' allows naming a feed and choosing an upload method. Products can also be imported directly from a Google Merchant Center account. The Shopify feed app route was used instead of the direct import. [L1036.003]

### Google Shopping variants

- Variant submission to Google Shopping: submit all variants if you want to run ads for all of them, or only the main variant if you want to run ads for one product. *Older advice (2021).* [L1090.034]

## Outsourcing Uploads

### Oversight and the first uploads

- Outsourced product uploads need full oversight, because a single misaligned row can put the wrong price on thousands of products. [L0359.037]
- Using Fiverr or external freelancers, never share your Shopify admin username and password. Generate a collaborator code instead, which gives limited access: freelancers can upload products but cannot access payment settings or restricted account areas. [L0106.004]
- Do not outsource product uploads blindly, because mistakes can make pricing wrong. Upload products yourself via CSV, and add a helper only after you can show the process by recording five to ten products. *Older advice (2022).* [L0665.025]
- Procedure to outsource product uploads for approved suppliers, in this order:
  1. Treat each approved supplier as its own separate project.
  2. Create one document per supplier with where its products are accessed, a link to its product feed, CSV or Excel file, and an SOP for the workflow.
  3. Upload the first 10 products yourself while recording your screen (a free tool such as Loom, Google Meet or QuickTime works), and add each step to the SOP as you do it.
  4. Note how long the 10 uploads took, as a baseline for the cost of hiring.
  5. Hire a freelancer on Upwork or onlinejobs.ph for the rest.
  6. Share the project folder with the SOP and recording with the freelancer.
  7. Spot-check every upload: product images must match the right product, and prices and titles must match the right product.

  [L0359.036]
- Procedure to hand off product uploads when supplier formats differ, *older advice (2021)*:
  1. Do a few uploads yourself to learn the process.
  2. Record your screen with Loom while uploading 10 to 20 products from one supplier.
  3. Send the recording to whoever will do the uploads.
  4. Treat each supplier as a separate job, because the work differs even when product counts are the same.

  [L0671.219]
- Loom is a free Chrome extension that records your browser, microphone and optional webcam with one click. Use it to record a sample task, such as uploading five products, then send the recording to a virtual assistant for training. *Older advice (2021).* [L0801.037]

### Freelancer procedures

- Outsourcing supplier CSV and product upload work when supplier files are messy, *older advice (2024)*:
  1. Search Upwork or Fiverr for a Shopify CSV expert.
  2. Send the freelancer your starting data (PDFs, spreadsheets) and tell them the exact format you need.
  3. Ask for a ready-to-upload CSV file.
  4. Review the CSV before uploading it.
  5. Do not give the freelancer access to your store.

  [L0806.029]
- Freelance product upload help can be found on Upwork by searching for Shopify product upload. Review the freelancer's reviews and past work before hiring, and check every upload for crossed details. *Older advice (no year given).* [L0941.032]
- Procedure for a supplier without a product list, *older advice (2021)*:
  1. Search the supplier's products on Google as a customer and check what other sellers charge.
  2. If other sellers mark them up only slightly, for example around 10 percent, drop that supplier.
  3. If margins are strong, record one product upload on video with a free screen recording extension and hire a virtual assistant to upload the rest.

  [L0801.036]

### How many products to upload yourself first

Sources differ on the sample size before outsourcing:

| Source | Year | Upload yourself first |
|---|---|---|
| L0663.073 | 2019 (older) | 5 products, then test import |
| L0665.025 | 2022 (older) | Record 5 to 10 products before adding a helper |
| L0359.036 | undated | First 10 products, recorded |
| L0671.219 | 2021 (older) | 10 to 20 products from one supplier, recorded |
| L1275.016 | 2019 (older) | Roughly the first 100 products, or all products from the first supplier |
| L0801.036 | 2021 (older) | One product upload recorded on video |

The 100-product figure is the oldest and largest. Use the 5 to 10 product test to start, because the sources agree that you should learn the process yourself before handing it off. [L0663.073, L0665.025, L0359.036, L0671.219, L1275.016, L0801.036]

### Quality checks and hiring

- Check every upload carefully, because wrong prices, images, descriptions or SKU numbers can get mixed between products. [L0941.031]
- Uploads of supplier products should be spot-checked on images, prices and titles before going live. The procedure is in "Outsourcing Uploads" above. [L0359.036]

## Costs of Manual and Outsourced Upload

### Pricing model by data format

Pricing depends on the supplier's data format. *Older advice (2019).*

| Supplier data format | Category | How to pay | Example from the lesson |
|---|---|---|---|
| CSV file (Excel-style sheet with products, prices, descriptions, image links) | Automated | Hourly rate or fixed project fee, never a fixed price per product | Formatting takes about 20 minutes to 1 hour regardless of whether there are 100 or 3,000 products |
| PDF, supplier website without a feed, or physical catalog | Manual | Fixed cost per product | About 5 minutes per product by hand |

[L1275.013]

- Paying a fixed price per product for a CSV feed overpays. Reformatting the file takes roughly the same time whether it has 100 or 3,000 products. *Older advice (2019).* [L1275.014]
- Manual upload hours = number of products x minutes per product / 60. For example, 3,000 products at 5 minutes each equals 250 hours of work, which is the basis for a fair per-product price. *Older advice (2019).* [L1275.015]

### Preparing Supplier Data

- Use one CSV file per supplier, because each supplier formats its data differently. [L1285.024.001]
- Open each supplier file and rename its columns to the exact headers of the Shopify product CSV template. [L1285.024.002]
- Add or delete columns until they match the template. [L1285.024.003]
- Copy and paste whole columns, not row by row. [L1285.024.004]
- Import the CSV for each supplier separately. [L1285.024.005]

### Rights to Photos and Price Lists

- Supplier photos and a price list are generally low risk to use when the supplier gives written permission, even without a signed contract. [L1283.012]
- Ask for that permission in writing before you publish any photos. [L1283.012]

### Product Identifiers (SKU, MPN, GTIN)

- Some products have no SKU or barcode. In that case, create an MPN with this procedure (older advice, from 2022):
  1. Take the abbreviation of the brand name, for example ABT for AB Tables. [L1281.013]
  2. Add a sequential number for each product, for example ABT123, ABT124, ABT125. [L1281.013]
  3. Enter this code as the manufacturer part number in the product data. [L1281.013]

### Choosing Which Products to List

- Once you are authorized to sell a brand, you do not have to list its whole catalog. [L1287.014]
- Choose the products that give you the best margin. These are often the higher-priced items. [L1287.014]

### Uploading to Shopify

- Use Shopify's native CSV import for bulk product uploads and edits. [L1285.025]
- Do not add a third-party bulk upload app for this. [L1285.025]

### Inventory Settings

- For dropshipping products, set the inventory quantity to 9999 so the product does not show as out of stock. [L1331.011]
- Confirm with the supplier that the stock actually exists, especially if you work with a private agent. [L1331.011]

### Shopify Setup for Meta Connections

- The Shopify setup screens for Facebook and Meta connections were recorded in late 2022, so menu names and button labels may have changed since then. [L1318.015]
- Match each step to the label that is actually on screen instead of following the recorded wording word for word. [L1318.015]

### Outsourcing Product Questions to Writers

Older advice, recorded 2021 to 2022: [L1396.070]

1. Send the manufacturer's contact an introduction email that names the writer and the page or blog being written. [L1396.070]
2. Tell the writer that they may call that contact directly with product questions. [L1396.070]
3. Keep these calls away from the project manager, who otherwise handles them on top of CSV uploads. [L1396.070]

#### Further details

- Ask suppliers for a CSV, Excel or product feed when possible, because a structured file makes product uploads much faster than manual entry. [L0359.032]

1. After approval, receive the product data, pricing and other information the supplier provides.
2. Load the product data onto your store.
3. Clean up the website design so the products look neat and consistent.
4. Check the store is complete and ready to take orders.
5. Launch the store and start the traffic funnel.
[L0458.040]

- When suppliers provide product data through a CSV template, make sure the product information in the file matches what should appear on your store. [L0468.053]

- Duplicate products from two suppliers, for example one at MAP and one without MAP, should not both be listed in the same store because it confuses customers. [L1113.026]

## Shipping Setup, Policies and Delivery Expectations

### Who Ships the Order: Supplier Account First

- The preferred arrangement is for the supplier to ship on its own account, so you receive one invoice, and the supplier handles returns, loss and breakage (older, 2019) [L0357.015].
- Ask suppliers to do this first, before you arrange any other shipping option (older, 2019) [L0357.015].
- Always have suppliers ship on their own accounts when possible. They negotiate better rates through volume and can handle tracking issues. Provide your UPS or FedEx account only if the supplier explicitly cannot ship from its own (older, 2021) [L0270.046].
- Ship through the supplier's own shipping account whenever possible, because suppliers ship high volumes and often receive bulk discounts that lower the cost (2025) [L0966.035].
- When possible, have the supplier ship from its own carrier account, because suppliers ship more volume and get better discounts. If a supplier will not ship on its account, the store must send bills of lading for freight shipments (2025) [L0667.019].
- Use the supplier's shipping account for shipping. Do not plan for freight carriers unless the supplier refuses to ship from its account (older, 2019) [L1069.015].
- Ask suppliers to ship on their own shipping account whenever possible, because it reduces liability and simplifies operations. If a supplier refuses, using your own freight account through a freight broker is still a workable option (older, 2019) [L1069.004].
- Prefer suppliers who ship on their own account, since they bill shipping on the invoice and handle damage claims and replacements. Take this option whenever it is available (older, 2018) [L0466.034].
- Prefer suppliers who ship from their own accounts because it keeps operations simpler. Bill of lading shipments are still acceptable when a supplier requires them (older, 2021) [L0469.022].
- Set shipments to go out on the supplier's account by default. If the supplier will not, get a freight quote and bill of lading, and have the shipment billed to the supplier (older, 2021) [L0834.019].
- Ship orders on the supplier's shipping account whenever possible. The supplier then handles damage insurance claims and can send a replacement right away (older, 2019) [L0663.038].
- Ship on the supplier's carrier account whenever possible, because suppliers ship high volume and usually get discounted rates. Use your own UPS, FedEx or freight accounts only when the supplier refuses (older, 2022) [L0665.015].
- If a supplier ships on its own account, use that setup because it means less work. If it will not, ship on your own account, which is also common (older, 2022) [L0803.016].
- Ship on the supplier's own account, with the supplier billing the store, whenever possible. Use this approach to keep operations simple (older, 2020) [L1088.011].
- Ship on the supplier's own account when it offers published or discounted UPS rates, because that makes dropshipping easier and often cheaper. Ask the supplier whether it has a discounted rate sheet for its carrier account (older, 2021) [L1090.028].
- Prefer suppliers with short shipping times, and ship on the supplier's own account whenever possible, since this is smoother for every order. Your shipping page can list all carriers you may use, and orders from different carriers are not a problem (older, 2021) [L0968.006].
- Ship on the supplier's account by default and count the shipping fee as a cost per order. Offering free shipping to customers means the store pays this fee to the supplier, so it is not actually free for the business. In the example it was 100 USD per order [L0759.017].
- Suppliers either ship on their own account and charge you for shipping, or they ship on your account. Your preferred option is for the supplier to ship on its account and bill you for shipping. If it does not allow that, you ship on your own account [L0933.018].
- Shipping on the supplier's account makes it faster for the supplier to print labels and ship the same day. It also makes claims simpler if an item is damaged or lost in transit, because the supplier can file the claim and send a replacement quickly [L0933.020].
- Template for asking a supplier to ship on its account (no year):

```
We would prefer to ship on your account and simply pay you for shipping. If that is not something you do, we can arrange shipping on our account, and we will give you our UPS or FedEx account number when we process orders.
```
[L0933.021]

- Suppliers that refuse to ship on your account are not a deal breaker, but work out who pays the carrier charge before accepting the order arrangement (older, 2022) [L0969.097].
- Ask suppliers which carriers their largest retailers use. Shipping on a supplier's business account usually gives bulk pricing (older, 2020) [L0468.038].
- Suppliers should ship directly to customers, not drop-ship on the store's behalf for their own products. For a US store, the supplier should be in the US. A foreign manufacturer is acceptable if its products are warehoused in the US (2023) [L0964.023].
- Ship on the supplier's own account, and do not send products to your own address first and then to the customer. Ask the supplier to ship directly to the customer's address (older, 2020) [L1111.039].
- Some suppliers, roughly 5 to 10 percent, require the seller to ship on their own account. In that case, ask for a UPS or FedEx account number to use for labels, or for a freight carrier the supplier prefers for freight orders (older, 2020) [L1088.012].
- Most suppliers do not pay shipping. When the store offers free shipping, the store pays that cost itself (older, 2020) [L1088.015].

#### Comparing the two account types

| Account used | Who bills the shipping | Invoice | Damage and replacement claims | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Supplier's account | Supplier bills you for shipping, usually as one invoice with the product | One invoice (product plus shipping) | Supplier files the claim and sends a replacement quickly | [L0357.015, L0969.008, L0663.038, L0933.020] |
| Your own account | Supplier charges only the product; the carrier bills the shipping separately | Two bills: supplier for product, carrier for shipping | You are liable for overage claims, and in-transit damage must be claimed from the carrier | [L0969.008, L0971.085] |

- Shipping on the supplier's account keeps damage and replacement claims with the supplier, and large freight is not shipped via UPS or FedEx. Insure shipments yourself only if damage is rare with that supplier (2024) [L0971.046].
- Seller accounts with UPS or FedEx are free to open (older, 2022) [L0969.008].
- Shipping on your own UPS or FedEx account makes you liable for overage claims, for example when declared measurements are wrong. In-transit damage must be claimed from the carrier rather than replaced free by the supplier (2024) [L0971.085].

### Using Your Own Carrier Account

#### Opening the account

- Do not open a shipping carrier account before contacting suppliers. Ask which carrier the supplier recommends, because suppliers know which carriers already collect at their warehouse at the best rates (older, 2021) [L1090.025].
- For standard UPS or FedEx shipments, create your own account with each carrier and give the supplier your account numbers, so it ships on your account rather than its own (older, 2022) [L0272.002].
- Create a free UPS or FedEx account online and share your account number with suppliers. They will use their own access to create shipping labels and charge the cost to your account. This requires almost no effort from you once set up (older, 2022) [L0249.091].

**Setup for small parcels when a supplier will not ship on its own account (UPS or FedEx) (older, 2022) [L0854.007]:**
1. Open a free account with ups.com or fedex.com.
2. Send your carrier account number to the supplier.
3. The supplier creates the shipping labels on your account for each order.

**Shipping options when a supplier does not ship on its own account (older, 2019) [L0357.016]:**
1. Give the supplier a FedEx or UPS account number so it ships on your account (both can be opened free online).
2. If that is not possible, ask the supplier which freight carrier or freight broker it recommends.
3. Partner with the recommended carrier or broker for those shipments.
4. For each customer order, place the order with the supplier after the sale, then use the chosen shipping method.

**When a supplier will not ship on its own account (older, 2019) [L0357.070]:**
1. Ask the supplier which shipping company or freight broker it recommends, since it already works with other retailers and knows which carrier handles most of its orders.
2. Contact the recommended carrier or broker.
3. Set up the account or partnership with that company for your shipments.

**Choosing a method with a supplier that does not ship on its account (2023) [L0251.022]:**
1. Contact the supplier and ask about its preferred shipping method.
2. If items are large and heavy, use a freight broker via freightcenter.com. The supplier may provide a freight account, or you create your own.
3. If items are standard parcel size, ask the supplier for its UPS or FedEx account number, or create accounts under your business name.
4. Communicate the chosen method clearly so both parties execute correctly.

- Ask suppliers who they recommend as a shipping company rather than asking whether they ship on their own account. Suppliers that do not ship themselves still know which carriers their other retailers use (older, 2019) [L0357.017].
- Ask which carriers the supplier recommends and which large retailers it uses. Choose carriers from that list (older, 2021) [L0247.019].
- Ask suppliers which carrier their largest retailers use, and open a business account in your own name (older, 2020) [L0468.038, L0468.039].

**Opening a business account for a better rate (older, 2020) [L0468.039]:**
1. Ask the supplier which carrier its top retailers use for shipping.
2. Contact that carrier and open a business account in your own name.
3. Tell the carrier where you ship from and that current pricing is not competitive.
4. Ask for a discount on the account.
5. Expect discounts to grow with shipping volume.

- Template for asking a carrier for a discount (older, 2020) [L0468.190]:

```
Hi [carrier contact name],

This is [your name] with [business name]. We have opened a business account with [carrier name] and we ship our products from [ship-from city, state].

The current shipping pricing is not competitive for us. Is there any discount you can apply to our account? We expect our shipping volume to grow, so we would also like to know how discounts increase as we ship more.

Thank you,
[your name]
[business name]
[phone number]
```
[L0468.190]

- Shipping discounts from carriers are usually based on volume. Higher volumes through a carrier, or shipping through a supplier who ships a lot on its own account, generally produce the best rates (2023) [L0835.032].
- Shipping services that charge a fee on top of carrier rates are only useful for stores with their own warehouse and high shipping volume. For drop-ship stores they are generally not worth the cost (older, 2020) [L0468.047].
- Before accepting a third-party group shipping discount, compare its rates against your actual shipping lanes. Some lanes may get better rates while others get worse. Do not assume a group deal is a good deal for everyone [L0942.027].

### Choosing Carriers and Comparing Prices

- For items that are not large, heavy or freight, use the carrier the supplier prefers, usually UPS or FedEx. Large, heavy items need a different carrier (2025) [L0857.030].
- Carrier choice for small parcels in Australia: Couriers Please, Sendle and eParcel were recommended. Compare their rates and delivery times before choosing one for a store (older, 2020) [L1087.001].
- In Australia, many brands rely on Australia Post because it is government-mandated to deliver nationwide. A hybrid setup with StarTrack, which Australia Post owns, can offer a branded tracking page on top of the carrier (Source E, 2025-26) [L0955.030].
- Pirate Ship is mentioned as a good option for small parcels. The lesson presenter recommended it from experience but had not used it personally (2025) [L0667.020].
- Pirate Ship is reported to be cheaper than UPS for small parcels, with insurance provided through a third party. Compare its rates against your own UPS quote before switching (2023) [L0835.140].
- Check shipping costs with a supplier's shipping calculator before committing, since international UPS shipping is typically very expensive. If the cost looks unreasonable to you, customers will likely see it the same way (older, 2021) [L0968.007].
- Sales reps at supplier brands are the best source for shipping advice. Ask which carrier they prefer, what pricing they get, and how their largest online retailers ship (older, 2020) [L0967.025].
- Before you use the same carrier as a supplier's other retailers, check its label status. If a supplier has a label pending status with FedEx for more than about 24 hours, call the carrier to check whether the delay is on the supplier or the carrier side (older, 2020) [L0967.032].
- If a supplier marks up shipping above its real cost, ship on your own account and do not pay the markup [L0359.027].
- If a supplier offers a better price when you handle shipping yourself, ask whether it means you buy the stock in advance or pay for shipping. If you ship with your own UPS or FedEx account, or through a freight broker's bill of lading, this can save money (older, 2020) [L0551.018].
- Ask the supplier whether it can use the same shipping method you select. If the price still does not match, shipping on your own account is the next step (2023) [L0835.024].

**Matching the carrier price with your own UPS account (2023) [L0835.025]:**
1. Ask the supplier to ship with the same shipping method you select, so the price matches your quote.
2. If it cannot match it, open a free UPS account for your business.
3. Give the supplier your UPS account number.
4. The supplier creates each shipment in UPS and enters your account number in the billing section.
5. UPS bills you for the shipping, and you pay the supplier only for the cost of the goods.

- Template for querying a supplier's higher shipping price (2023) [L0835.026]:

```
Hi [Supplier contact name], we were reviewing the quotes for our shipments and noticed yours are substantially higher than the ones we get from UPS. Just wanted to make sure you are choosing the same shipping method, or are we missing something here? Could you check whether you can use the shipping method we select so the price matches? If not, we would like to ship on our own UPS account, so please use our account number [UPS account number] when you create shipments.
[Your name]
[Company name]
```
[L0835.026]

- Supplier surcharges for shipping on their own account (for example a 5 dollar surcharge on regular items and 25 dollars on large items) are a reason to compare with self-arranged carrier quotes before accepting (older, 2022) [L0969.010].
- Shipping on the supplier's account versus on the seller's account: when the supplier ships on its own account, it charges the product plus shipping in one invoice. When the seller ships on its own UPS or FedEx account, the supplier charges only the product, and the carrier bills the shipping separately (older, 2022) [L0969.008].

**Negotiating shipping costs with a supplier (older, 2020) [L0852.042]:**
1. Bring real numbers to the supplier, including the number of sales, where orders shipped, shipping costs and the margin you are left with.
2. Ask whether the supplier can ship through its preferred carriers on its own account, which often gives better rates.
3. Ask for incentivized pricing or a wholesale discount so the product becomes profitable for both sides.
4. If the supplier ships through freight, get separate freight quotes from freight brokers and compare them with the supplier's rate.

- Template for asking a supplier to cover shipping on its account and cut costs (older, 2021-22) [L1046.034]:

```
Hi [Supplier name], last year we spent about [98,000] with you on shipping and fulfillment. Your shipping volume is likely higher than ours, so could you check whether your freight accounts or carrier rates can cover our orders at a lower cost? Our target for next year is about [75,000], and I would like to agree on a plan to reach it. Could we set up a call this week to review the rates? Thank you, [Your name].
```
[L1046.034]

- Some suppliers do not coordinate shipping, or their shipping is too expensive, so the store may use a separate freight provider such as UPS. In that case product cost and shipping cost must be tracked separately, and both need to be compared against the supplier's pricing (older, 2021-22) [L1046.029].
- Record each supplier's shipping and fulfilment details so an assistant can arrange pickups, including supplier pickup times, warehouse hours, the contact person, and which carriers collect from the supplier's area [L1000.051].

### Insurance, Damage and Claims

- Insure every shipment. Insurance is the baseline protection, regardless of which carrier is used, and it stops occasional damage from costing the store money (older, 2021) [L0853.002].
- Insure every shipment, whether the supplier ships it or you ship from your own shipping account, so damaged or lost goods can be replaced without an out-of-pocket loss. Insurance is selected as an option when the shipment is created (2025) [L0966.030].
- Shipments sent to customers should be insured against loss and transit damage. Suppliers usually insure shipments made on their own accounts (older, 2020) [L1088.013].
- Insure shipments yourself only if damage is rare with that supplier. If the supplier ships on its own account, it handles claims (2024) [L0971.046].
- Before paying extra for specialized shipping on fragile goods, check each supplier's products for complaints (older, 2021) [L0853.001]:
  1. Search Google for the supplier's name and product names together with "reviews".
  2. Look for repeated mentions of returns, refunds or damage from other stores' customers.
  3. If no clear pattern appears, ship with a recommended carrier and insure every shipment.
  4. Only use costlier specialized shippers if damage remains a recurring problem.
- Check the carrier's insurance terms before relying on insurance. Some policies exclude parcels shipped in manufacturer boxes printed with product names or SKUs, which can lead to denied damage claims (older, 2021) [L0247.020].
- Insurance is covered by the supplier when it ships on its account, so the store can focus on tracking the claim and the replacement [L0663.038].

### Tracking and Multi-Supplier Shipments

- Require suppliers to send a tracking number when an order ships. A supplier that offers no tracking at all is a risk, so ask it to at least reply to purchase orders with tracking numbers (older, 2021) [L0247.022].
- In-stock orders should have tracking information sent within 24 hours of the order leaving the warehouse. If the customer has none after four business days, they should follow up by email (older, 2021-22) [L0556.024].
- Customers accept multiple packages if they receive tracking numbers. When customers order products that ship from multiple suppliers, send tracking information for each shipment as it ships separately (2024) [L0276.022].
- Handling a multi-item order shipped from different suppliers (older, 2020) [L0852.038]:
  1. Expect the items to ship separately from different warehouses, and tell the customer this is normal.
  2. When part of an order ships, enter the tracking number in the Shopify order backend for only the products that shipped.
  3. Keep the customer updated so each separate shipment is expected and understood.
- When a store's products come from several suppliers, supplier-specific shipping costs can make some products unprofitable. Check shipping cost and the supplier's location before deciding where to advertise (older, 2020) [L0967.044].
- Free tracked shipping instead of standard shipping can increase trust and reduce checkout drop-off (Source C) [L0640.038].
- Weekly shipments for bulk ordering (Source C) [L0324.012]:
  1. Collect all orders placed Monday to Friday.
  2. On Friday, batch-ship all week's orders to DHL in one consolidated shipment rather than individual drops.

- Regional assignment of suppliers reduces shipping times. Instead of one centralized warehouse, assign suppliers by region: Northeast suppliers handle Northeast orders, Southeast suppliers handle Southeast orders. This speeds delivery without increasing costs (2025) [L0278.040].

### Free Shipping and Pricing It In

#### Deciding whether to build shipping into the price

- Offer free shipping on all products as the base option. Carrier costs are paid by the business, and rates are checked after orders come in rather than calculated at checkout (older, 2021) [L0247.034].
- Free shipping is paid out of the seller's own margin, so sell a product under a free-shipping threshold (for example under 3,500) only if the margin covers the shipping cost. Shipping fees vary by supplier (2025) [L0472.022].
- Because the store offers free shipping on everything, shipping cost must be built into product prices so each order still leaves a minimum of 25 percent net profit (older, 2018) [L0466.008].
- If a supplier can't be priced to cover shipping, restrict its products in advertising to nearby regions (older, 2018) [L0466.009].
- If you offer shipping charged to customers, decide whether to build it into the price or charge at checkout by checking what the top 2 or 3 competitors do for the same brand (2023) [L0804.036].
- Free shipping is the usual standard in these niches, so it is not a differentiator. Check what the top two to five competitors charge for shipping and price your products to match, since those stores are your direct rivals on Google Shopping (2024) [L0965.038].
- Free shipping from suppliers is a plus when launching a store with a few suppliers (2024) [L0836.044].
- When a supplier says shipping is free, confirm whether the shipping cost is built into the product price, because that changes the margin calculation [L0359.028].
- Passing international shipping costs to the buyer is possible only if the final price still works for the product. Shipping should not cost more than the product itself (older, 2020) [L0852.048].
- For heavy items, avoid real-time shipping calculation in the store. Build the shipping cost into the product price and offer free shipping, provided the product is still profitable (older, 2019) [L1068.030].
- Shipping that a supplier charges directly to the customer can be handled by adding the cost to the product price or by creating a separate Shopify shipping profile for that supplier's products (2024) [L0971.049].
- Some suppliers require the seller to create and send a shipping label for pickup, so the store pays that shipping cost directly. For heavy items, check this cost before listing, because it can exceed the margin and make the product unprofitable (2024) [L0836.067].
- When a competitor advertises free international shipping for a bulky or heavy product, the cost is either built into an expensive price or the seller stocks the same product in warehouses in both countries. Cross-border shipping of large or heavy goods is very costly, so a claim of truly free shipping only works for very high-priced, small items. Check which explanation applies before copying the model (older, 2021) [L1090.144].
- Free shipping is excluded for Hawaii and Alaska because it is too expensive to offer for free (older, 2021) [L0834.031].

#### Calculating an average shipping cost (2018 method)

1. Note each approved supplier's shipping origin and where your customers are located, for example the share of sales per state.
2. Ask each supplier for shipping quotes for its most popular product to those regions, ideally while applying for approval.
3. Calculate an average shipping cost per product, weighted by the share of your business in each region (for example a 50 percent split between two regions).
4. Add that average into the product price instead of marking the price up more for shipping.
5. Check that the resulting net margin still meets the 25 percent minimum while free shipping is offered.
6. If the margin still does not cover shipping for a supplier, restrict that supplier's products in advertising to nearby regions (see geo-targeting).

(older, 2018) [L0466.009]

- Example: a stand-up paddle board costs about 50 dollars to ship within the same state and about 150 dollars from a supplier in a distant state. If half of the customers are in each zone, the average of 100 dollars is the figure to build into the price (older, 2018) [L0466.010].
- If a product's shipping cost can consume nearly all of its net profit per order, for example 150 dollars of profit against 150 dollars of shipping, do not sell it to the expensive regions. This situation should be rare (older, 2018) [L0466.011].
- If a supplier cannot quote shipping for individual items, ask for package specifications (dimensions and weight of each item and box) in a spreadsheet or CSV and estimate costs from those numbers (older, 2018) [L0466.013].
- Template for requesting shipping quotes from a supplier (older, 2018) [L0466.069]:

```
Hi [Supplier name], we have been approved and are preparing our store. Most of our customers are in [State A], about [X]% of our business, and we also sell to [State B]. What would it cost to ship your most popular product, [product name], to a [State A] address and to a [State B] address? Please include the package weight and dimensions with each quote.
```
[L0466.069]

- Estimating a typical shipping cost from a supplier's warehouse (2025) [L0966.034]:
  1. Get the zip code of the supplier's warehouse.
  2. Request sample shipping quotes from that warehouse to several random US zip codes, for example New Jersey to New York, Florida, Texas and California.
  3. Average the quotes to estimate the typical shipping cost per order.
  4. Use this average, not a single quote, when calculating margins.
- Track the shipping cost against the margin before you cut prices. Shipping costs can quickly erase margin on heavy items, so apply the calculated average, not a single quote, when setting prices [L0966.034].
- Shipping costs can jump sharply when a product crosses into a higher shipping size class, so even a one-inch increase in length can matter. Ask suppliers for the dimensions that define each shipping class and the packing materials they use before deciding how to box or group products (older, 2022) [L0963.155].
- Enter accurate product shipping dimensions for every item, especially large ones. Wrong sizes can make shipping costs higher than needed [L1000.062].
- Enter package sizes such as box dimensions in Shopify so that carrier-calculated shipping rates are accurate (Source D, 2026) [L0798.016].
- Branded mailer boxes can be sourced from a friend's business in exchange for free packaging, with the parcel carrying that business's branding as part of the arrangement. This lowers packaging cost in the early phase of a small brand (Source C) [L0492.065].

### Shopify Setup: Locations, Rates, Zones and Profiles

#### Locations and origin address

- Settings > Locations: keep the default location unchanged and do not add supplier addresses. The location represents the business's billing location, not the suppliers [L0096.014].
- Shipping origin address in Shopify: enter your business address or main fulfillment location. With free shipping offers (no shipping cost charged to the customer), this address does not affect shipping rate calculations and is primarily for documentation purposes (older, 2019) [L0243.001].

#### Shipping rates and free shipping

- Settings > Shipping and delivery: create one domestic shipping rate with the country United States and the rate Free shipping. Customers then see Free Shipping at checkout. This setup applies when the store offers free shipping [L0096.012].
- In the shipping rate editor, the Offer free shipping option makes a rate free, and checkout then shows the rate name with FREE instead of a price. A paid rate uses rate type Flat with a Price field (Source D, 2026) [L0798.020].
- Settings > Shipping and delivery has a Shipping labels section for buying discounted shipping labels and managing preferred carriers. The Carrier accounts section lets you connect an existing carrier account so its own rates are used at checkout (Source D, 2026) [L0798.017].
- Settings > Shipping and delivery > Additional delivery methods offers Local delivery and Pickup in store as options alongside regular shipping. The Sender name on shipping labels field is in the same section (Source D, 2026) [L0798.018].

#### Shipping profiles and product templates

- Shopify shipping profiles segment which products ship from which regions or suppliers. Multiple profiles allow assigning Supplier A products to specific states and Supplier B products to others (2025) [L0278.006].
- The Shipping profiles section has an Add custom profile button for creating an extra profile for specific products, which can have its own zones and rates. Products not assigned to a custom profile stay in the General profile, which is labeled as covering all products not in other profiles (Source D, 2026) [L0798.019].
- Under Settings > Shipping and delivery > General profile > Packages, enter package sizes so that carrier-calculated shipping rates are accurate. The Packages field sits in the same profile view as the shipping zones and estimated delivery dates (Source D, 2026) [L0798.016].
- Different suppliers have different shipping speeds and require different delivery windows. Create separate product templates for each supplier group, assign the correct template to products, and set estimated delivery to match that supplier's actual timeline [L0139.008].
- Set a shipping profile for each supplier that lists its made-to-order lead time (see the made-to-order section below) [L0856.053].

#### Customs information in Shopify

- The Customs information section in a Shopify product takes a Country/Region of origin, which is usually where the product is manufactured, and an HS (Harmonized System) code that can be searched by keyword. Border officers use these fields to calculate import duties on international orders, and they appear on the customs forms printed during fulfillment. Demo products can skip this section (older, 2021-22) [L0452.027].

### Shipping Coverage and Geography

#### Alaska, Hawaii and the continental states

Options for Alaska and Hawaii, from the sources, which conflict. Each applies to a different store setup:

| Option | When it applies | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Charge shipping to Alaska and Hawaii; offer free shipping only to the continental 48 states, and state this on the shipping policy (2020) | When you sell to these states at a shipping charge | [L0852.040] |
| Exclude Alaska and Hawaii from the free shipping offer (2021) | When free shipping is offered, because free shipping is too expensive to offer there | [L0834.031] |
| Exclude Alaska and Hawaii from checkout and do not run ads there (2022); state on the shipping page that quotes for those states are available on request by email | When shipping to these states is not cost-effective | [L1092.040] |
| Exclude Alaska and Hawaii from advertising at the start, because shipping costs there differ from the continental US. The continental states generally do not need to be excluded (2025) | When launching ads, to avoid unprofitable regions | [L0966.031] |

- For a US store, the shipping region in the shipping policy is set to Continental United States only. Large and heavy items are usually shipped as freight, so the freight option is set to yes for them (Source A) [L1048.015].

#### Regional supplier assignment and multi-state profiles

- See regional supplier assignment and shipping profiles above [L0278.040, L0278.006].

#### Warehouses in different countries

- When a supplier stocks both a US warehouse and a China warehouse, US stock usually ships faster to US customers. Route orders to US stock where the product is available, and compare delivery times on both warehouses before choosing (Source C) [L0524.071].

#### Unsupported countries

- For a country your supplier will not ship to, check the real shipping cost by entering the item's dimensions, weight and destination address before selling it (older, 2019) [L1002.063].
- Shipping surcharge test for a country your supplier will not ship to (older, 2019) [L1002.064]:
  1. Track the actual extra cost per order, for example from reshipping through a relay address, and calculate the average.
  2. Add a surcharge for that country at about the average. Example: average cost $100, surcharge set to $120.
  3. Watch whether orders continue. If they do, the surcharge covers the cost. If they stop, you avoided losing money on shipping.

#### Country-specific notes

- Fast delivery sells more in markets like Spain. Delivering within about 48 hours helps conversion, and cheap prices matter most for buyers there (Source C) [L0361.039].
- Cross-border export: before shipping products from suppliers in other countries to your customers, ask each supplier how it handles export and cross-border shipping, because the process varies by supplier (older, 2019) [L0663.058].
- For products made abroad with long delivery times, state on the product page where the product is made, what happens after the order, and the estimated delivery time, for example four weeks. Clear disclosure prevents most complaints (older, 2019) [L1086.034].

### Handling Time, Transit Time and Delivery Estimates

#### Definitions

- Handling time is the number of days between the customer placing an order and the item being processed and ready to ship, not the shipping duration. Dropshippers do not control this, so a single-day handling time is risky and a window should be used instead (older, 2021-22) [L0731.011].
- Delivery expectations should separate average handling time, average transit time and typical total delivery time, each stated in business days. Giving all three figures sets accurate expectations for large purchases and reduces refund requests caused by surprise delays [L0541.004].

#### Delivery windows by source

The sources give different windows. Each applies to a different supplier and niche:

| Source | Handling time | Transit time | Total delivery | Applies when | Source ids |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Source B, dropshipping store (older, 2021-22) | 1 to 3 days, Monday to Friday only | 1 to 7 days, adjustable over time | Not stated | Most dropshipping stores; very few suppliers ship on weekends | [L0731.012, L0731.013] |
| Source B, in-stock orders (older, 2021-22) | Typically 1 to 3 business days | 1 to 6 business days after shipment | Not stated | In-stock items | [L0556.024] |
| Source A, starting placeholders (no year) | 3 to 5 business days | 3 to 7 business days | 7 to 12 business days | Until supplier timelines are known; can be changed later | [L1048.014] |
| Source A, shipping policy (older, 2020) | Dispatch 1 to 2 days, depending on each supplier's cut-off | 1 to 7 days (up to two days dispatch on the long end) | Not stated | Standard parcels; freight takes longer | [L0852.046, L0852.047] |
| Source A, shipping policy (older, 2021) | Dispatch within 48 business hours | 2 to 5 business days | Not stated | General policy average; supplier-specific times go on each product page | [L0469.034] |
| Source A, example supplier (older, 2021) | Supplier needs 2 to 3 days, stated as ships within 72 hours | Tracking follows by email | Not stated | Per-supplier product page, when a supplier needs 2 to 3 days | [L0853.017] |

- Do not promise delivery times that are too fast or too slow. Overpromising leads to angry customers when delivery is late, while very long transit times such as 20 to 30 days on average put buyers off (older, 2021-22) [L0731.014].
- The estimated delivery date shown to customers is calculated from the order time (before or after the order cutoff), the handling time and the transit time. Google adjusts the estimate automatically when a public holiday falls within the delivery period (older, 2021-22) [L0731.036].
- The order cutoff time in a Merchant Center shipping service is optional. If it is used, set it to the business's local time zone, for example 5 PM in GMT+10 for a business in Brisbane, Australia (older, 2021-22) [L0731.037].
- Most suppliers should dispatch orders within one to two days. Learn each supplier's daily order cut-off time, since orders placed before the cut-off ship the same day and later orders ship the next day (older, 2020) [L0852.046].
- Same-day shipping can be offered as an upsell only if the supplier can ship the order by a fixed cutoff time, for example an order placed by noon Eastern time. Suppliers that ship in around three days cannot support this offer without that cutoff [L0923.047].
- Record each supplier's time from order submission to dispatch and state it on that supplier's product pages. For example, if a supplier needs 2 to 3 days, tell customers the item ships within 72 hours and that tracking follows by email (older, 2021) [L0853.017].
- Add processing days to the advertised shipping window. Counting only the advertised shipping days with a supplier underestimates delivery time. Long processing times with a supplier like CJ can lead to angry customers, especially around holidays or long weekends (Source C) [L0678.023].
- Carrier delivery estimates can shift by several days or even a week during the holiday catch-up period, especially with domestic shipping. Set customer expectations around a range and keep the tracking ETA visible rather than promising one fixed delivery date (older, 2022) [L0803.098].
- Keep the product page estimate for lead time or shipping visible and clear. Customers usually accept longer delivery times when it is stated openly (older, 2021) [L0671.037].
- Delivery information can be shown as an estimated date range or as typical shipping time. Choose the format that fits your products; custom-made items need different wording than items shipped within 48 hours (older, 2021) [L0469.036].
- Put a general average shipping time in the shipping policy, such as dispatch within 48 business hours and delivery within 2 to 5 business days. Add the supplier-specific lead time on each product page's shipping tab (older, 2021) [L0469.034].
- Customers used to fast delivery may see 10 business days as slow, so a 14-day supplier route still carries refund risk. Delivery times of 4 to 6 weeks cause refunds and lasting problems, so avoid those routes (Source C) [L1108.044].
- Fashion dropshipping: keep communication with the supplier very good and make sure products deliver within about 10 days. Slow delivery increases refund and dispute risk (Source C) [L0660.013].
- Long shipping times can kill viral momentum. Standard dropshipping delivery can take around three weeks, by which time buyers and the seller have often lost interest (Source C) [L0706.029].
- When shipping times are a problem, check eBay for the same product and consider paying extra for faster shipping. Paying more on CJ Dropshipping or AliExpress has sometimes produced delivery within about one week (Source C) [L0706.030].
- Customers respond well to fast shipping, so supplier and fulfillment choices should prioritize short delivery times. Fast delivery is a competitive advantage in dropshipping [L0345.002].

#### Claims about speed

- Claims of same-day or one-day shipping on dropshipping stores are usually unrealistic, and many stores show three to five days shipping. Plan delivery times and customer messaging around realistic supplier shipping times instead of copying competitor claims (Source C) [L0372.025].
- A competitor stated that most orders are dispatched within 2 hours of being placed. This dispatch-time claim works as a delivery promise. Record stated dispatch times, together with tracking and shipping claims, in the features and deliverables column (older, 2021-22) [L0893.042].

#### Lead times and long supplier routes

- Lead times of 3 to 6 weeks are acceptable only if your top 2 to 3 competitors have the same lead time. If competitors ship in 24 hours and you ship in 3 to 6 weeks at the same price, customers will buy from competitors. Clearly display the lead time on product pages and confirm it with customers after order placement (2024) [L0253.025].
- A supplier with a lead time of several weeks is not automatically a problem if that is standard in the niche. Show the delivery time on the product pages, for example two to three weeks, so customers know what to expect (older, 2021) [L0968.013].
- Long shipping times are a problem only when they are not standard for the niche. In custom-made product niches, customers accept longer waits, but the custom order process should be clear to buyers before they purchase (older, 2021) [L0968.109].
- Prefer suppliers with short shipping times. (See the supplier-selection rules above) [L0968.006].
- CJ offers about six or seven different shipping methods. Shipping time depends on the supplier and the method agreed (Source C) [L1108.038].
- CJ Sensitive shipping to Australia takes about 7 to 15 days. The speaker used it as the default shipping method for these markets because the delivery time was acceptable to customers (Source C) [L0661.016].
- The fastest CJ shipping method typically takes 5 to 11 days when shipping from the US. It costs more than the standard method, so use it only when the extra cost is small (Source C) [L0661.017].
- Pay for faster shipping only when the extra cost is small, such as one dollar or a few cents per order. Do not pay several dollars more per order, because it eats up the margin (Source C) [L0661.018].

#### Made-to-order products

- Made-to-order products: set a shipping profile for that supplier listing the lead time (for example six to eight weeks), show the ETA by the add-to-cart button, and set the same in Merchant Center. Email each customer after the sale to confirm the made-to-order lead time before sending the order to the supplier (2024) [L0856.053].
- Made-to-order fulfillment steps (2024) [L0856.054]:
  1. Create a Shopify shipping profile for that supplier that marks items as custom made with the lead time.
  2. Mirror the same shipping lead time in Merchant Center for that brand.
  3. Show a note by the add-to-cart button that the item is made to order with its ETA.
  4. After each sale, email the customer to confirm the made-to-order lead time before the order goes to the supplier.

### Google Merchant Center

- For freight-shipped products in Google Merchant Center, list the shipping method as free shipping with a price of 0 dollars, rather than naming the freight method (2024) [L0471.055].
- Google's shipping estimate uses the handling time, transit time and cutoff, and adjusts for holidays. See Handling Time, Transit Time and Delivery Estimates above [L0731.036, L0731.037].

### Heavy and Freight Items

- For heavy items, avoid real-time shipping calculation in the store. Build the shipping cost into the product price and offer free shipping, provided the product is still profitable (older, 2019) [L1068.030].
- If real-time shipping is not used for heavy items, a heavy-item shipping surcharge can be added automatically on the product page. For items of 150 to 350 pounds, the surcharge should match the real shipping cost, for example $200 extra for a 350 pound item, and must be checked against actual costs (older, 2019) [L1068.031].
- For a freight-shipped item, use a freight broker through freightcenter.com, with the supplier providing a freight account or the store creating its own [L0251.022].
- Freight shipments should not go through UPS or FedEx, because their rates are too high for large items (2024) [L0971.085].
- If a supplier will not ship on its own account, the store must send bills of lading for freight shipments (2025) [L0667.019].
- Freight shipments take longer than standard parcels (older, 2020) [L0852.047].
- Shipping for heavy products can be a large cost. Check the shipping cost and label arrangement before listing (2024) [L0836.067].

### Customs, Cross-Border and International Shipping

- See Customs information in Shopify above [L0452.027].
