- Before shipping products from suppliers in other countries to your customers, ask each supplier how it handles export and cross-border shipping, because the process varies by supplier (older, 2019) [L0663.058].
- Passing international shipping costs to the buyer is possible only if the final price still works for the product (older, 2020) [L0852.048].
- International UPS shipping is typically very expensive. Check the cost with the supplier's calculator before committing (older, 2021) [L0968.007].

### Shipping Policy Text

#### Policy brief (full prompt)

Paste the full brief below into ChatGPT or any other AI writing tool, then add your own answers to the fields at the end. The result is a finished, professional Shipping Policy that can go straight into Shopify, reads as clear and dependable, and is tailored to your brand.

```
The assistant should act as an eCommerce operations copywriter producing a transparent, friendly policy for a Shopify store that sells high-ticket products sourced from domestic suppliers. The policy should reassure buyers, set realistic delivery expectations, come across as seasoned and reputable, avoid robotic or boilerplate phrasing, and speak to ordinary shoppers rather than legal readers. The word 'dropshipping' must never appear. Voice: clear, professional, reassuring, trust-building, straightforward. Format: plain paragraphs under short headers, 300 to 500 words, no filler and no hype, just clarity and confidence. Required headers, in this order: Shipping Coverage Area, Order Confirmation, Order Processing Time, Shipment Notification & Tracking, Estimated Delivery Time, Shipping Carriers, Delays & Exceptions, Contact Information. The overall impression should be that of a premium company that treats logistics seriously. Fields to complete: Store Name [e.g. Coastal Ridge Living]; Support Email Address [e.g. support@coastalrideliving.com]; Shipping Region [e.g. Continental United States only]; Average Handling Time [e.g. 3-5 business days]; Average Transit Time [e.g. 3-7 business days]; Typical Total Delivery Time [e.g. 7-12 business days]; Shipping Partners [e.g. FedEx, UPS, USPS, Freight Carriers]; Do You Ship Freight for Large Items? (Yes/No) [e.g. Yes]; Do You Offer White-Glove or Curbside Delivery? (Optional) [e.g. Curbside delivery available for oversized items]; How You Handle Out-of-Stock or Custom Orders [e.g. Customer is contacted with updated timelines]; How You Handle Delays [e.g. We proactively notify customers if delays occur]. Closing instructions: build a complete policy that feels established, states delivery timing accurately, earns trust for big-ticket purchases, and can be pasted into Shopify as is. Each generation should come out differently. Leave out any placeholders, skip explanations and notes, and output only the finished policy. Carry over the field answers exactly as entered. If the optional field is blank, drop that topic completely rather than inventing details. Return just the ready-to-paste policy copy, with every header alone on its own line.
```
[L0541.001]

#### Building the policy

- Build a Shopify shipping policy for a high-ticket store with domestic suppliers in this order [L0541.002]:
  1. Shipping Coverage Area
  2. Order Confirmation
  3. Order Processing Time
  4. Shipment Notification & Tracking
  5. Estimated Delivery Time
  6. Shipping Carriers
  7. Delays & Exceptions
  8. Contact Information
- A shipping policy for high-ticket products should stay between 300 and 500 words, use simple paragraphs and short section headers, and avoid hype and legal wording. Write it for real customers so it reads as a premium, established brand that takes logistics seriously [L0541.003].
- A shipping policy pasted into Shopify must not contain bracketed placeholders, notes, or the word "dropshipping". Leftover template text or the word itself makes the store look unfinished and damages trust in a high-ticket purchase [L0541.005].
- Starting values for the policy's delivery figures are in the delivery windows table above [L1048.014].
- Use the policy fields to state: the shipping region (continental United States only), handling time, transit time and total delivery time, each in business days [L1048.015, L0541.004].

### Speed, Expectations and Competitor Claims

- See Claims about speed and Lead times above [L0372.025, L0893.042, L0253.025].
- Customers are sensitive to delivery times, so set expectations clearly and keep them realistic [L0731.014, L0660.013].

### Common Mistakes

- Counting only the advertised shipping days with a supplier underestimates delivery time [L0678.023].
- Long shipping times can kill viral momentum [L0706.029].
- Shipping on your own UPS or FedEx account makes you liable for overage claims, and freight should not go through UPS or FedEx [L0971.085].
- Do not have a supplier ship products to your own address first and then to the customer. Ask the supplier to ship directly to the customer's address (older, 2020) [L1111.039].
- Do not open a carrier account before contacting suppliers [L1090.025].
- Do not assume a group shipping discount is a good deal for everyone [L0942.027].
- Do not assume shipping on your own account is cheaper without comparing it with supplier quotes [L0551.018, L0835.024].

### Shipping Model: Supplier Account or Own Account

- Ship through the supplier first, since suppliers usually offer the best shipping rates. If the supplier marks up shipping, ask for better rates or another service, then compare quotes from several shipping companies using the same shipment details. (Source A, 2020, older.) [L1277.035]
- Choose products that suppliers can ship on their own account when they allow it. If an item is damaged or lost, the supplier sends the replacement and handles the insurance claim. (Source A, 2018.) [L1274.035]
- If you ship on your own account, you must file the insurance claim yourself and wait to be repaid by the carrier, which is slower. Damage is rare, but repeated damage from one supplier's products means that supplier is bad. (Source A, 2018.) [L1274.036]
- Suppliers ideally ship on their own accounts, but some will only ship on yours. This is common and should be checked with a test shipment before the first order. (Source A, 2021, older.) [L1279.029]
- Keep a shipping arrangement table with one row per supplier, as in the example below. Check it before placing any order, because it decides whether the supplier or your business books the shipment. [L1291.001]

| Supplier | Shipping |
|---|---|
| Bobs Brown Chair | Supplier books shipping |
| Super Seats | We book shipping using UPS |
| Crazy Chairs | Supplier books shipping |

[L1291.001]

### Carriers and Who Books the Shipment

- Ask a supplier which carrier they use for their top retailers, such as FedEx, UPS, DHL or a freight broker, and send shipping details accordingly. Ship to the customer's address from the supplier's account. (Source A, 2020, older.) [L1111.040]
- When a supplier arranges shipping, it books pickup and delivery with its own nominated carrier after you place the order. You only need to pass the customer's delivery address and phone number to the supplier. [L1291.002]
- For suppliers whose shipping you arrange yourself, ask the supplier for the package weight and dimensions before booking, because the carrier needs both. The supplier sends these details together with its invoice for the order. [L1291.003]
- Shipping to the customer from a supplier with poor shipping and thin margins: test different warehouse locations and freight companies, and add more suppliers with better margins. Those products can then sit in the profitable group that the budget supports. (Source A, 2024.) [L1285.026]

### Free Shipping and Pricing It In

- Free shipping is the default offer in this dropshipping model because competitors offer it and customers expect it. The seller does not get shipping for free; the seller pays the supplier and absorbs that cost. [L1180.004]
- Free shipping is offered to customers, and the supplier is paid the actual shipping cost. The store does not calculate shipping through Shopify in this model. (Source A, 2020, older.) [L1111.049]
- Free shipping is offered only to the continental United States, not to Alaska, Hawaii or international destinations, because shipping there is very expensive. In other countries, international shipping is usually too costly to offer for free or to make available at all. [L1180.005]
- Free shipping is an offer many stores overlook. Shoppers who want to save money will choose a free-shipping store over a similar store that charges delivery, so the store can absorb shipping costs to win more customers and stand out from competitors. [L1400.009]
- Shipping to Canada from US-based suppliers is usually fine with free shipping, but at the start put most ad budget into US sales, since those produce the highest profit. (Source A, 2020, older.) [L1277.043]
- Offering free shipping worldwide is only sustainable if the shipping cost is covered by the product margin or the supplier. Add the free shipping claim only after checking the cost per country. [L1250.024]
- Shipping cost limits, two sources, both apply to the same goal:
  - Keep shipping costs at a maximum of 10 dollars, or make shipping free by building it into the product price, especially for lower-margin products under 50 dollars. [L1260.050]
  - For products under $50, keep shipping at a maximum of about 10% of the product price, or offer free shipping by including the shipping cost in the product price. (Under $50, 10% is at most $5, so the percentage is the tighter limit for those products. The $10 cap is the reference for higher-priced items.) [L1345.069]
- Expedited shipping should cost no more than 15-20% of the product price, and high expedited fees should be avoided. [L1260.050, L1345.069]
- Expedited shipping can be marked up for profit, but only offer it with suppliers who respond very quickly. If your supplier usually takes a day or two to respond, a second-day delivery promise puts you in a bad position. (Source A, undated.) [L1357.023]
- Free shipping and white glove can be communicated on the product page, at the product price and near the add-to-cart button. Showing the estimated arrival date next to free shipping builds trust at the point of purchase. (Source A, 2022, older.) [L1281.006]
- Ship trial or free-examination products from the warehouse or branch nearest the buyer, with shipping paid in advance. A shorter delivery distance shortens the time before the trial starts and keeps the buyer from losing interest. Prepaid shipping also removes a reason to delay. [L1369.124]

### Shopify Setup: Locations, Zones, Profiles and Rates

- Shopify shipping settings must contain at least one shipping rate before launch, and a free shipping rate is the minimum required. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L1145.015]
- Shipping must be set up before launch, because a shipping zone with no rates stops customers in that zone from completing checkout. The zone shows 'No rates. Customers in this zone won't be able to complete checkout' until at least one rate is added. [L1167.015]
- Shopify splits shipping into a Domestic zone for your home country and an International zone that covers the other countries in your active markets. Each zone needs at least one shipping rate, because a zone without rates stops checkout for customers located there. [L1167.039]
- International shipping zones with no rates block checkout for those countries. Shipping rates and active markets must both be in place for each country you want to sell to. [L1167.022]
- Under Settings > Shipping and delivery, one shipping policy is created for the domestic country, United States, with shipping set to free. Free shipping then appears in checkout for US buyers. (Source A, undated.) [L1306.017]
- The location list under Shipping and delivery should contain only the store's own billing address as the single location, with everything else left at default. [L1306.019]
- Supplier addresses should not be added as Shopify locations. Only the store's own business location belongs there. [L1306.020]
- Default domestic shipping rates in the example were $7.50 and $5.00. Delete these default rates under Settings > Shipping and delivery > General shipping rates before adding your own rates. [L1167.016]
- Procedure: Set up shipping rates in Shopify:
  1. Go to Settings > Shipping and delivery and click Manage on the shipping profile.
  2. Delete the default rates Shopify adds.
  3. Create a custom flat rate named 'Free shipping' with a price of 0.
  4. Add an express option, for example 'Expedited shipping' at $4.99.
  5. Repeat for the rest of the world with a suitable rate.
  6. Optionally create special zones such as ePacket or T4 for Canada, Australia, and the United Kingdom, with their own price.
  
  [L1345.068]
- For a free shipping offer, create a flat rate named for the offer (for example 'Free shipping') with a shipping rate of 0 and apply it in the zone. If you offer free shipping to both rate types in the domestic zone, set both to 0. [L1167.017]
- For free shipping above a set order value, such as $50, use 'Add conditional pricing' on the rate instead of a flat 0 rate. Choose the rate type 'Use flat rate' and enter a custom rate name. [L1167.018]
- The default package is a 'Sample box' of 22 x 13.7 x 4.2 cm and 0 kg. It is used to calculate shipping rates at checkout and is pre-selected when buying labels, so replace it with your real package size and weight. [L1167.019]
- All products not assigned to another shipping profile go into the General profile, and newly created products are added there automatically. Create a new shipping profile only when certain products need different rates or destination restrictions. [L1167.020]
- Expected delivery date options are Shop Promise (automated delivery dates for eligible orders), Manual delivery dates (shown for orders without the Shop Promise badge), and Local delivery (off by default, with a Set up option). [L1167.021]
- Procedure: Setting up shipping zones (Settings > Shipping and delivery > General profile):
  1. Open the general profile for all products.
  2. Create separate zones for the top four markets: Canada, Australia, United States and United Kingdom.
  3. Put the remaining countries into a fifth zone (the lesson's example lists Germany, Europe, New Zealand and other countries).
  4. Add the countries to each zone.
  
  Lumping countries into one zone is acceptable, but separate zones are easier to read and manage. [L1327.022]
- Procedure: Settings > Shipping and delivery > General profile > Add shipping option for Express Shipping: choose the Express shipping option, set Rate type to Flat, set the price to $0.00 and enter the number of business days for delivery. The estimated dates at checkout are calculated from transit time and fulfillment time. [L1327.023]
- Express Free Shipping is always used: the shipping cost is not charged to the customer and is instead included in the product price, calculated from the average order value of the bundle sold. Shipping is therefore set as a flat $0.00 at checkout. [L1327.025]
- Shipping rules and shipping costs can be set up later and do not need to be finished when building the first product page. Shipping only becomes a detailed task when you add shipping rules or shipping costs. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1331.012]

### Handling Times and Dispatch

- Separate processing time from delivery time in the shipping policy: processing covers how long until the order ships, delivery covers how long until it arrives. State both as business days. [L1250.022]
- For an in-stock order, a store can state that it ships within three business days of the order date and sends a shipping confirmation within 24 hours after the parcel leaves the warehouse. If the customer has no shipping confirmation within four business days, they can follow up with the store by email. These timings belong on the shipping page so customers know the expected window. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L1264.018]
- Some suppliers can dispatch quickly. CJ Dropshipping and similar suppliers can ship orders within about 24 hours when the product is in stock. A private agent holding local stock can also ship within 24 hours or less, so a fast dispatch promise can be stated on the store when the supplier is verified to be that quick. [L1259.067]
- Suppliers such as CJ Dropshipping, or a private agent holding stock, can often dispatch within about 24 hours, which can support a 1-2 day dispatch promise on the product page. Confirm dispatch times with the supplier before making that promise. [L1260.070]
- When the supplier ships within 24 hours, state dispatch time as 'We ship within one to two days'. Use the real dispatch time when writing shipping copy, for example CJ Dropshipping or a private stock agent typically ships within 24 hours or less. [L1345.049, L1345.050]
- Dispatch times differ by source, so use the one your supplier confirms:
  - In-stock orders with a 3-business-day ship window, 24-hour confirmation and 4-business-day follow-up. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L1264.018]
  - Verified fast suppliers (CJ Dropshipping, private stock agents) at about 24 hours, supporting a 1-2 day promise. [L1259.067, L1260.070]
- Source D's example template processes orders within 14 business days and moves orders placed after 12 PM PST, or on weekends or holidays, to the next business day. This is a template value and should be replaced with your supplier's real figure. [L1327.060]
- Orders are prepared within [X-X] business days, with weekends and public holidays not counted. A confirmation email with tracking details arrives once the parcel has left the store. [L1250.006]
- Before promising a shipping time or a return window in your policy, confirm that your supplier can meet it. A policy the supplier cannot fulfil creates refund requests and complaints. [L1250.021]
- Shipping policy text is expected to change after suppliers and products are in place, because delivery times and costs depend on the local shipping area and the country the store operates in. Adjust the shipping page at that point rather than treating the first version as final. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L1264.011]

### Delivery Estimates and Expectations

- Use the default product template for estimated delivery when all suppliers ship in a similar time, and create a separate template for each supplier whose delivery window differs, such as one that takes 7 to 14 days. (Source A, undated.) [L1192.024]
- Show shipping times on the page, for example 7 to 14 days, and mention the express shipping option. Clear delivery times reduce doubt before the purchase. [L1194.048]
- Advertising a delivery time you cannot meet leads to complaints and refunds. Test orders with China-based suppliers reportedly took about 20 days while quoted 4 to 9 days, so only advertise a delivery time you have measured. [L1214.008]
- Delivery within about 10 days is generally acceptable, because customers wait when the product and service are good. A Prime-like 1-2 day service only matters at high daily volume from one country. [L1217.008]
- Express carrier lines come in tiers: Priority typically delivers in about 3-4 days, Standard is the normal choice, and Economy can take 15-18 days. Delivery of 3-5 days is considered good, and even 5 days is acceptable. [L1217.012]
- Domestic shipping from a local supplier typically takes two to four days, unless a product is on backorder, in which case it can take longer. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L1481.015]
- Enter the delivery window in business days based on the fulfillment provider's real transit time: a US 3PL uses 12 to 21 business days (or 10 to 14 business days for a faster setup), while a China 3PL needs a longer window because transit is longer. Do not promise shorter times than the fulfillment center can deliver. [L1327.024]
- Delivery windows in the sources differ by setup and are not interchangeable:
  - Source D template: Standard 14 to 21 business days, Express 7 to 10 business days. [L1327.060]
  - Source C copy template: delivery in 7 to 10 business days. [L1345.048]
  - Source D US 3PL: 12 to 21 business days, or 10 to 14 for a faster setup. [L1327.024]
  - Domestic local supplier: 2 to 4 days. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L1481.015]
- Logistics and customer service matter as much as finding a winning product, because shipping and service failures directly hurt sales and repeat purchases. Plan these areas before scaling ad spend. [L1251.033]
- In dropshipping, the supplier ships each order directly to the customer, often from overseas such as China, so the store owner cannot control shipping speed. Because of this, delivery time and working tracking matter a lot for customer trust and repeat purchases. [L1251.050]
- Shipping times can damage influencer partnerships, so choose suppliers with fast shipping before starting influencer marketing. [L1184.027]
- Prefer light, non-fragile products when choosing a product. In one case, 4 kg items shipped to the UK, Australia and the US without customs problems. [L1217.014]
- Set the customer-facing delivery window with a positive lead-in. Use this copy on the product page and in checkout, replacing bracketed placeholders:

```
Your order is on its way! We ship your item within [1-2] days, usually within 24 hours, from our [supplier or stock location]. Delivery takes [7-10] business days to your door, and you will receive your tracking number within [12-24] hours of shipping. Due to high demand, delivery times may vary slightly during peak periods, but we will keep you updated every step of the way.
```

  [L1345.048]
- Rules for that copy: open with the positive shipping promise, never state a delay without a reassuring line, and use 'We pay for shipping' instead of 'free shipping' when shipping is free. Give the delivery window such as 7 to 10 business days with a positive lead-in and tracking within 12 to 24 hours. [L1345.048, L1345.049]
- Phrase free shipping as 'We pay for shipping' on the store. [L1345.049]
- For a holiday promotion that promises delivery before Christmas, the supplier must be able to deliver within about two weeks. Confirm this before running the promotion. (Source A, 2025.) [L1287.018]
- Source D worldwide policy template gives both estimates in one line: Expected arrival: domestic orders [X-X days], international orders [X-X days]. Dispatch happens within [X] business days. When the parcel goes out, you receive a tracking number to follow it. Customs and weather can push back delivery dates. [L1250.036]

### Tracking

- To give customers a local tracking number instead of a China-origin number, follow this procedure:
  1. Ask the supplier or agent to ship with a local carrier such as USPS, Royal Mail or Australia Post, and to hide the origin information.
  2. Ask them to send the local number to Shopify automatically once the courier issues it.
  3. If no local number appears quickly, send the original tracking number and ask them to hide the origin.
  
  The local number often appears the same day or in a few hours for destinations like UK, Germany, Australia and New Zealand, but may only appear on arrival in the destination country for others. [L1315.004]
- A courier tracking number that is only the first leg (for example a China number) may not show delivery progress or days left. The local carrier number is needed for that, and tracking with a local carrier like FedEx or DHL shows the remaining days. [L1315.005]
- Once an order ships, the customer receives a tracking number by email so they can follow the package. [L1250.025]
- Procedure for Google Merchant Center shipping: (Source A, 2020, older.)
  1. Check the Google Merchant Center shipping settings.
  2. Locate the free shipping entry.
  3. Enter the average delivery timeframe for the whole catalog.
  
  The delivery time applies catalog-wide, not per product. [L1111.051]

### Multi-Supplier and Add-On Shipments

- Products sold as add-ons or upsells should come from the same supplier as the main product, so the order ships together without an extra shipping fee. Items from another source ship separately and need their own handling. (Source A, 2020, older.) [L1277.041]
- Use a separate delivery template for each supplier whose window differs from the default. [L1192.024]
- Each supplier's shipping arrangement must be recorded separately in the table above, because each one may book its own carrier. [L1291.001, L1291.002]

### Shipping Insurance and Damage

- Shipping insurance should always be included on every shipment. When the supplier ships from its own account, insurance is typically the default and the seller should confirm it in advance. (Source A, 2022, older.) [L1281.009]
- Insurance and replacement responsibilities follow the account used: a supplier shipping on its own account handles replacements and claims, while a store shipping on its own account must file and wait for repayment. [L1274.035, L1274.036]

### Customs, Duties and Cross-Border Shipments

- When a product is shipped from stock in the same country, there are no extra import fees for the customer. Customs clearance only matters for cross-border shipments. (Source A, 2022, older.) [L1281.007]
- For cross-border shipments, carriers such as FedEx, UPS and DHL usually handle customs clearance and contact the customer when duties must be paid. In practice the seller typically pays those fees. Some carriers were not verified for this. (Source A, 2022, older.) [L1281.008]
- Delivery times can be affected by customs, weather and other factors outside the store's control. Naming these in the policy sets expectations and reduces complaints about delays. [L1250.026]
- For international orders where product dimensions vary widely, the store can give shipping prices on request rather than publishing one rate. The customer confirms the quote before the order and payment are taken through a secure online payment gateway. This approach suits low-volume stores that cannot yet predict shipping cost per destination. (Source B, 2021-22, older.) [L1264.021]
- Tell customers that international delivery can take longer than the stated estimate and that you only ship to selected countries. Customers should also receive a tracking number by email once the order ships. [L1250.025]
- Customers pay customs fees, taxes and import duties on international orders, and the policy should state this. [L1327.060]

### Shipping Policy and Customer-Facing Text

- Shipping rates are calculated at checkout based on the customer's location and the shipping option chosen. The policy should say this instead of listing fixed prices that may not match the checkout. [L1250.023]
- Shipping policy template (replace bracketed placeholders; add the free shipping line only if margins and supplier costs cover it):

```
Thanks for buying from [Your Store Name]. We aim to get your order to you fast and reliably. Details follow.

1. Processing time
- Orders are prepared within [X-X] business days, with weekends and public holidays not counted.
- A confirmation email with tracking details arrives once the parcel has left us.

2. Shipping rates
- Checkout works out the cost from your address and the delivery method you choose.
- Worldwide delivery is free. [Keep this line only if your margins and supplier costs cover it.]

3. Estimated delivery
- Once processed, orders usually reach you within [X-X] business days.
- Transit can vary with location and outside factors we can't control, like customs clearance and weather.

4. International shipping
- We deliver to a limited list of countries. Overseas transit may be slower than the estimate given.
- When your order dispatches, a tracking email follows so you can watch its movement.

5. Shipping issues
- For any delivery problem, contact [your contact email]. Our team will help with tracking and make sure the package gets to you as planned.
```

  [L1250.006]
- Full shipping page template from the Source D example (replace bracketed text and the {placeholders}; the example store offers only express free shipping at this stage, so remove the standard line if you do not offer it):

```
Welcome! At {Store name} we are committed to getting your orders to you quickly and efficiently. Please read our shipping policy carefully to understand how we ship.

Shipping Rates
Shipping charges are calculated from the weight and dimensions of your order and the shipping method you choose. Rates are shown at checkout.

Shipping Methods and Delivery Times
We offer several shipping options to suit your needs:
- Standard Shipping: delivery within 14 to 21 business days.
- Express Shipping: delivery within 7 to 10 business days.
Delivery times are estimates and may vary depending on your location and external factors. [Adjust these windows to match the delivery times set in Settings > Shipping and delivery. The lesson's store offers only express free shipping at this stage, so remove the standard line if you do not offer it.]

Order Processing Time
Orders are processed within 14 business days.
Orders placed after 12 PM PST, or on weekends or holidays, will be processed on the next business day.

International Shipping
We ship worldwide. International shipping rates and delivery times differ by destination.
You are responsible for any customs fees, taxes or import duties.

Order Tracking
Once your order ships, you will receive a tracking number by email so you can follow your package.

Lost or Damaged Packages
If your package is lost or arrives damaged, contact our customer service team at {Business email} within 7 days of the delivery date for help.

Incorrect Address
Please make sure your shipping address is correct before you place your order. We are not responsible for packages delivered to an address the customer provided incorrectly.
```

  [L1327.060]
- Worldwide Shipping Policy: the tracking line in the sample template reads "Once your order ships, you will receive a tracking number by email so you can follow your package." [L1327.060, L1250.025]

## Freight, Heavy Items and White Glove Delivery

### Why heavy items change the margin

- Large and heavy products cost more to ship, which reduces the profit left per sale. When you compare a cheap product with an expensive one, include the shipping cost in both. [L0474.003]
- Large and heavy items need a separate shipping calculation, because their weight- or dimension-based cost can change a product's margin far more than it does for small items. [L0430.011]
- Large or bulky products such as home decor can be expensive to ship. Calculate the shipping cost against the ad price and margin before testing, because shipping can make a product that looks profitable in the ads unprofitable in practice. [L0638.058]
- Large and heavy products usually ship at freight rates rather than standard parcel rates. Confirm weight and dimension thresholds with carriers before you set the selling price. [L0675.011]
- Heavy and bulky products stay profitable only if the margin can absorb freight costs. Compare quotes from several companies and check the supplier's preferred carrier first. [L0967.039]
- Heavy items can cost more to ship than the margin allows. Example: a 60 lb item may cost 160 USD to ship. Calculate the average freight cost before you list and advertise the product. [L0836.040]
- Example of shipping-driven pricing: a 430-dollar shipping cost on an order that earns 90 dollars of margin makes the item unprofitable. [L0964.044]

### Check shipping cost before listing or advertising

#### Set a price floor by category

- Older (2019): For heavy or bulky items, ask suppliers for average shipping costs to the markets you plan to serve before choosing a product. Set a minimum price threshold so the margin covers shipping, and do not list cheaper items in that category. [L0357.019]
- Older (2019) example: If a heavy item such as a gun safe costs about $250 to ship, a $200 product cannot be sold profitably. If the lowest profitable price is $600, every item below $600 is excluded, and customers who want cheaper items are left to competitors. [L0357.020]
- Older (2020): If a brand's terms require a freight shipment on your account and the product margin works without MAP, freight is worth doing. Ask the supplier which freight company it recommends and work with that company. [L0358.047]
- A 30% markup on heavy products may not cover shipping costs. Shop freight quotes from multiple carriers instead of accepting the supplier's shipping quote. [L0276.025]
- Older (2022): For a large or heavy product with nationwide shipping of 100 to 150 dollars (up to 200 to 250 dollars for huge items), selling at a low price is not profitable with free shipping. [L0665.067]
- Older (2019): Carriers such as freight companies can cost around $150 per shipment, which makes items priced at $250 to $300 unprofitable. Ask each supplier which carrier it ships with. [L0663.044]

#### Estimate a whole range of products

Procedure: estimating shipping costs for many supplier products without quoting each one (2024)
1. For each product, calculate the spread as retail (MAP) minus wholesale cost in a spreadsheet, and sort from highest to lowest spread.
2. Look at the products with the smallest spread at the bottom of the sheet.
3. Ask the supplier for package size and weight for those 20 to 30 low-spread products.
4. Get free shipping quotes for those products from freightcenter.com.
5. Products where the shipping cost would consume the margin are the ones not to list.

[L0666.024, L0666.025]

Procedure: estimating shipping cost for heavy items before pricing (2024)
- Enter the supplier warehouse address and several made-up customer addresses across the country into freightcenter.com.
- Read the average shipping cost per item from the results.
- Set the selling price so the product stays profitable.

[L0836.039]

Procedure: estimating whether a large, heavy product is worth selling before you have supplier prices (older, 2022)
1. Assume a wholesale cost, for example half of the expected selling price.
2. Ask the supplier candidates for the box size and weight of the product.
3. Get shipping quotes using these dimensions and weight (no account login needed).
4. Check whether the spread after shipping is big enough to leave a solid margin.

[L0854.023]

Procedure: get estimated freight rates before orders come in (2024)
1. Use a free instant freight quote site such as freightcenter.com.
2. Enter the supplier's zip code as origin.
3. Enter several customer zip codes in different regions, for example one near the supplier, one on the East Coast and one in the West or South.
4. Compare the quotes to estimate shipping costs.

Exact costs need both addresses and the package size. [L0806.024]

Procedure: average freight cost for free-shipping pricing (older, 2021)
1. Get the supplier's typical product size and weight.
2. Use a freight quote tool such as freightcenter.com for freight shipments.
3. Set the ship-from address to the supplier's warehouse, for example New York.
4. Get quotes to ship-to addresses in several regions, for example New York, Florida, Texas and California.
5. Average the quotes and build that cost into the price of free shipping.

[L0801.039]

### Get freight quotes and compare them

#### Why quotes differ

- Freight brokers are approximately 50% cheaper than UPS or FedEx for large and heavy items. [L0251.010]
- Using only one freight carrier can lead to overpaying by about $100 to $200 per large shipment. Comparing quotes from several carriers can recover that difference. [L0854.006]
- Different quotes for the same shipment often differ by $100 to $300. [L0852.043]
- Older (2019): Freight prices can differ by hundreds of dollars between providers. Use several freight brokers for large freight. [L1086.037]
- Older (2019): Accepting the first freight quote is costly, because overpaying on freight can cost hundreds of dollars per shipment. Compare several brokers before shipping large goods. [L1086.039]
- Older (2021): The same carriers are quoted with different markups on different platforms. Compare the quotes from providers such as Freight Club and Freight Center on every order, because one can be cheaper by about $100. [L0834.018]
- Older (2021): Large, heavy products shipped from a single distribution point can vary by $150 or more between regions. Use separate shipping calculators for the supplier's location and average costs from several destinations, for example California, New York, Texas and Florida. [L0834.017]
- Freight quotes are free, and prices differ for the same shipment. Get quotes from several freight brokers. Freight Center is a common starting point, and Blue Freight Forwarder is another option members often use. [L0941.030]
- Freight quotes from brokers such as freightcenter.com and Red Hawk can differ. Collect quotes for the same shipment from several broker websites. [L0966.036]

#### Freight broker tools

- Freight Center (freightcenter.com) gives instant freight shipping quotes without binding you to a carrier. Enter package size, weight, origin ZIP and destination ZIPs to compare rates from multiple freight carriers and calculate actual shipping costs. [L0251.011]
- FreightCenter.com aggregates freight quotes from multiple LTL (less than truckload) carriers in real time, so you can compare prices before you pick a shipment method. Freight Club offers the same service with potentially better pricing. Use these when suppliers require you to arrange shipping on your own account for large or heavy items. [L0272.001] (older, 2022)
- Use Freightcenter.com before launching ads to check that margins survive real freight costs. [L0276.014]
- Freightcenter.com provides free instant shipping quotes. Enter the supplier's warehouse zip code, package size and weight, and a few US destination zip codes to see real freight estimates. [L0666.025]
- Use Freight Center to get sample quotes before the first sale, and to check whether a supplier's freight quote is too high. [L0667.018]
- Freight Center is a freight shipping service for items too large or heavy for standard parcel carriers. Use it to get freight quotes when pricing bulky products. [L0675.010]
- Older (2019): Freight Center is a starting point for comparing large-shipment quotes. Ask your suppliers which freight companies their other retailers use, because this gives insider information on what works. [L1086.038]
- Older (2021): Freight Center is a resource for handling shipping and freight matters for dropshipped orders, especially bulky or heavy items. [L1089.008]
- Older (2022): Freight broker freightcenter.com lets you calculate freight rates by entering the supplier warehouse, the customer's warehouse or address, box size and weight. It is useful for heavy items where shipping costs are high. [L0969.011]
- Older (2022): FreightCenter is used when a supplier will not ship on its own account for large, heavy products. It collects less-than-truckload quotes from many freight carriers that compete for the shipment, which helps find the lowest price. [L0854.004]
- Freightcenter.com lets you get instant freight quotes from different companies and compare them with larger carriers. Freight carriers bid on shipments, so prices can vary widely. [L0835.030]
- Free online freight quote sites take one shipment's details, such as size, weight, origin and destination, and return prices from several carriers. These sites do not own trucks, so comparing their quotes shows the going rate for large or heavy items. [L0853.113] (older, 2021)
- Older (2020): Freight Shipping Estimates is a listed resource in the December 2020 coaching call for estimating freight shipping costs. The lesson names it without describing how to use it. [L0426.006]

#### Source conflict: are automated calculators available?

- Older (2018): No automated shipping calculator exists for this purpose. Shipping averages are built from supplier quotes, and freight broker tools such as FreightCenter.com are used for large or heavy items without quotes. [L0466.015]
- Later sources (2023): No automated real-time freight price calculator was known in the lesson. For large and heavy items when suppliers do not ship on their own account, get freight quotes through a freight broker such as freightcenter.com. [L0934.042]
- Later sources (2022 to 2024) name broker tools that return quotes online, including FreightCenter. [L0272.001, L0276.014, L0666.025]
- Use the 2018 statement only as history. The 2022 to 2024 sources describe the current practice. [L0466.015, L0934.042, L0272.001, L0276.014]

#### Procedures for getting quotes

Procedure: getting freight quotes through FreightCenter (Calculate freight rates, 2024)
1. Go to freightcenter.com.
2. Click 'Get Quote'.
3. Select 'Less Than Truckload'.
4. Enter your supplier's zip code.
5. Enter representative destination zip codes across the country (for example: New York, Florida, Texas, California).
6. Input estimated package weight and dimensions.
7. Collect freight quotes.
8. Calculate an average to estimate your typical shipping cost.

[L0276.015]

Procedure: getting freight rates on FreightCenter (2022)
1. Open freightcenter.com and click 'Calculate freight rates' at the top.
2. Select less than truckload (LTL), since the order does not fill a full truck.
3. Enter the supplier's warehouse location, the package size and weight.
4. Enter the customer's delivery address.
5. Compare the quotes from the carriers and choose the best price.

[L0854.005]

Procedure: using the Freight Center rate calculator
1. Open freightcenter.com and click Calculate freight rates.
2. Enter the supplier warehouse zip code as origin.
3. Enter destination zip codes around the country, such as New York, Florida, Texas and California.
4. Enter package sizes and weights for less-than-truckload shipments.
5. Click View my rates to see estimates.

[L0941.029]

Procedure: estimating freight rates with FreightCenter's 'Calculate Freight Weights' (older, 2018)
1. Get the dimensions and weights of the product packages from the supplier.
2. Open FreightCenter.com and choose Calculate Freight Weights.
3. Enter the city or zip code where the supplier ships from as the origin.
4. Enter destination zip codes in the regions where your customers are, testing for example a New York, a California and a Florida zip code.
5. Enter the packaging details and box dimensions and weight given by the supplier.
6. Enter the freight class if known, otherwise leave it as unknown.
7. Click to get quotes and note the prices for each destination to build your averages.

[L0466.014]

Procedure: building an average rate from supplier products (older, 2020)
1. Ask the supplier for the top three best-selling products, with weight and packaging dimensions.
2. Open a freight rate calculator and set the supplier's warehouse as the origin.
3. Enter the dimensions and weight of each product as the shipment.
4. Enter several destination addresses across different regions, for example one in the origin state, one in Florida, one in Texas and one in California.
5. Average the results to get a ballpark rate.
6. Repeat with more suppliers until the rates are known from experience.

[L0468.040]

Procedure: getting a freight quote through freightcenter.com for an item shipped from a supplier warehouse (older, 2021)
- Step 1: Enter the supplier warehouse address as origin, the customer address as destination, and the package size and weight.
- Step 2: Compare the quotes and choose one.
- Step 3: Accept the quote to receive a bill of lading (BOL), which states the shipment route.
- Step 4: Send the BOL to the supplier, who attaches it to the package.
- Step 5: The freight carrier, often an LTL (less-than-truckload) company, picks up the goods and delivers them to the customer.

[L1090.026]

Procedure: getting shipping quotes for heavy or bulky items (older, 2022)
1. Obtain box dimensions and weights from the supplier (not product dimensions).
2. Request quotes from UPS or FedEx, or from a freight broker such as freightcenter.com, by entering supplier warehouse, customer destination, box size and weight.
3. Compare the supplier's shipping charge with your own quotes. Suppliers shipping high volume may get better rates.

[L0969.009]

Procedure: getting freight quotes from brokers (2024)
1. Open a free account with a freight platform before you need quotes.
2. Get quotes from several freight brokers, and tell each one you are comparing quotes.
3. Ask brokers for a discount, since they have room to negotiate.
4. Use a free instant quote on freight platforms such as Freight Center, choosing the LTL service.

[L0855.063]

Procedure: contacting freight brokers for the best rate (2024)
1. Open a free account with a freight platform.
2. Email the platform with a competing quote if a supplier's shipping quote is cheaper.
3. Ask whether a better price can be offered.
4. Repeat with several quotes. Pricing usually improves after a few tries.

[L0855.064]

Procedure: contacting freight brokers (2024, the 'ask for a discount' steps)
1. Contact 4 to 5 freight brokers.
2. Provide each one with the item weight, dimensions, supplier zip code and destination zip code.
3. Mention that you are comparing rates for ongoing freight needs.
4. Collect and compare the quotes.
5. Negotiate rates or select the best pricing.
6. Repeat for different regions to determine average costs.

[L0276.026]

Procedure: shipping heavy or palletized freight (2025), for example 90 to 100 pounds on a pallet
- Do not open a UPS account, since parcel pricing is absurd for pallets.
- Use a freight broker such as freightcenter.com, which quotes several freight carriers at once.
- Enter the quantity as one pallet, the product weight, the supplier's ZIP code and the customer's delivery ZIP code, then compare the quotes.

[L0472.020]

#### Checking a supplier's freight quote

- Suppliers usually have the best shipping rates because they move high volume. To test a freight quote, send the supplier's shipping quote, with supplier details removed, to one or more freight brokers and ask them to beat it. [L0805.025]
- Older (2023): If a supplier will not ship on its own account, compare its freight carrier quote with a freight aggregator quote. Differences of $100 to $200 per shipment are possible and add up over time. [L0970.037]
- Freight aggregators collect quotes from many carriers for heavy items when you enter origin, destination, package size and weight. A quote that seems extremely high, such as over $1,000 for a 250-pound item crossing from Canada to the US west coast, should be checked before you accept it. [L0970.040]

Procedure: checking supplier freight quotes (2023)
1. For each new order, ask the supplier to send a quote from its freight carrier, giving the order number, product and customer shipping address.
2. Enter the pickup location, destination, package size and weight into a freight aggregator such as Freightcenter.com.
3. Compare the aggregator quotes from different carriers with the supplier's quote.
4. If the supplier's carrier is in line with the market, use it to save a step. If the aggregator is clearly cheaper, book that option.

[L0970.038]

Template to send to the supplier with each order (2023):

```
Hi [supplier contact], here is our next order: order number [number], product [product name], shipping to customer [customer name] at [full shipping address]. Please send a quote through your freight carrier so we can compare it with our own freight quotes before you ship.
```

[L0970.039]

- For a large list of products with different weights and sizes, quote one by one at the start. Once you have a relationship with a freight broker, send a spreadsheet of shipment data and group items with similar sizes and weights to get quotes faster. [L1090.027] (older, 2021)
- Ask the supplier which freight company it prefers and whether it can introduce you. Different suppliers have different freight relationships, and this often gets the best price. [L0852.043]
- Shipping through the same freight company for years without comparing quotes can cost a lot of money. The lesson's example was thousands of shipments, with savings of $100 to $300 per shipment once quotes were compared. [L0852.044] (older, 2020)
- Ask competitors who they ship with when they offer free shipping that seems unprofitable. Then approach their carriers or your own suppliers for better shipping rates or purchase order discounts. [L0834.016] (older, 2021)
- Negotiate freight with brokers. Ask brokers for a discount, since they have room to negotiate. [L0855.063]

### Choose who books the freight: supplier account or your own

#### Supplier account

- Some suppliers allow you to ship on their account. They charge you for product and shipping together. Others require you to ship on your own account. [L0255.011]
- The ideal setup for freight: the supplier quotes the order, creates the shipment and the Bill of Lading (BOL), and charges shipping on its own carrier account. The store does not set up freight shipping in Shopify. [L0854.003] (older, 2022)
- For large and heavy items such as furniture, it is normal for the supplier to ask the retailer to ship on the retailer's own shipping account. Expect this request when a supplier names a shipping account. [L0470.018]
- When suppliers ship on their own account, they typically get volume discounts on freight, so the shipping cost is better than you can negotiate alone. [L0255.012]

Procedure: freight shipping when the supplier ships on its own account (2024)
1. The supplier books the freight carrier and sends a tracking number when the shipment is ready.
2. You forward the tracking number to the customer.
3. The freight carrier contacts the customer to schedule delivery, for example on a specific day between 12 and 3 pm, since large heavy items are not dropped at the door.

[L0805.022]

Procedure: freight shipping with a supplier that ships only to you (2024)
1. Get quotes from a freight broker for the shipment.
2. Choose the price and carrier you want.
3. The broker issues a bill of lading (BOL).
4. Send the BOL to the supplier.
5. The supplier arranges with the trucking company to pick up the goods at the warehouse and deliver them to the customer.

[L0806.022]

#### Your own account

- Shipping on your own account makes you responsible for anything that happens in transit. Accept it only for large or heavy items when no other option exists, and do not write the supplier off immediately. [L0466.036] (older, 2018)
- Some suppliers ship on their own account, and some require you to ship on yours. When you do, arrange the shipment as described below. [L0255.011, L0255.013]
- If a supplier says it will not ship on its own account, ask which of its top retailers use which carrier. Those retailers usually have the best rates. Contact that carrier to arrange pickup at the supplier's warehouse. [L0854.008] (older, 2022)
- Ask the supplier to send a quote and to check whether its carrier is competitive. Use the supplier's carrier if it is in line with the market. [L0970.038]

Procedure: freight shipping when you ship on your own account (not UPS or FedEx) (2024)
1. Create the shipment through a freight broker such as freightcenter.com.
2. The broker generates a BOL (Bill of Lading), a document describing the shipment from the supplier warehouse to the customer address.
3. Send the BOL to the supplier.
4. The supplier arranges pickup at its warehouse with the freight shipper, who delivers to the customer.

[L0805.023]

Procedure: arranging freight shipping on your own account (2025)
1. Shop online for freight quotes from multiple carriers.
2. Compare pricing across different quotes.
3. Select the quote you prefer.
4. Pay for the shipment.
5. Receive a BOL (Bill of Lading), which shows pickup location (supplier warehouse) and delivery location.
6. Email the BOL to the supplier.
7. The freight company contacts the supplier to arrange pickup date and time.
8. The freight company picks up the order from the supplier warehouse.
9. The shipment gets a tracking number and delivers to the customer.

[L0255.013]

### Pick the carrier type: parcel or freight

#### Parcel carriers for small items

- For small parcels, UPS and FedEx usually have the best rates. Their freight rates for large items are often well above what freight brokers quote, so for freight use freight brokers and ask them about discounts. [L0835.033]
- For normal-size parcels, UPS and FedEx are reasonable. [L0972.099]
- Older (2019): Small parcels with UPS, FedEx or USPS usually cost about the same. The freight-broker advice in this section applies only to freight. [L1086.037]
- Ask each supplier which carrier it ships with. UPS, FedEx or DHL keep shipping affordable for compact products. [L0663.044] (older, 2019)

#### Freight for large and heavy items

- Large and heavy items are shipped by LTL (less than truckload) freight from the supplier's warehouse to the customer, not with UPS or FedEx. [L0855.061]
- Large and heavy items are far too expensive to ship with UPS, FedEx or DHL as parcels, so they move through freight brokers instead. Parcel carriers are only used for smaller products. [L0854.002] (older, 2022)
- For products above a certain size and weight, shipping with UPS or FedEx becomes too expensive, so you ship with a freight carrier. Freight is the standard option for large and heavy items. [L0961.032] (older, 2020)
- For large and heavy items such as 9 x 57 x 36 inches and about 70 pounds, use LTL freight shipping instead of parcel carriers like FedEx and UPS. [L1085.009] (older, 2018)
- Heavy items of 300 to 400 pounds usually ship as LTL freight, which is expensive. Get freight quotes from a site such as freightcenter.com by entering the supplier warehouse, package size, weight and destination. Negotiate rates, since there is often room. [L0804.035]
- Freight-shipped items for a product of about 100 to 350 pounds can be quoted up to $600 per shipment as LTL. Compare quotes from several freight companies before setting shipping prices. [L0667.017]
- Less-than-truckload freight quotes for heavy or long-distance orders can be very high, for example 500 to 600 USD per shipment. Check whether a product can still be sold profitably before advertising it. [L0966.037]
- For large and heavy furniture and similar bulky products, freight shipping is usually handled as LTL freight. Aggregator sites that handle heavy shipping cover this category. [L0970.041]
- Use the 'UPS or FedEx for normal parcels, freight for large' split: for large and heavy items, use traditional freight shippers, since the freight rates of UPS and FedEx are far higher. [L0972.099]

#### UPS and FedEx freight

- Never use UPS Freight or FedEx Freight for freight items. Their rates are far too expensive for heavy or large freight orders. [L0806.023] (2024)
- UPS and FedEx are not used for freight shipments because they are too expensive. For freight, the shipment is created through a freight broker and a BOL is generated. [L0805.024]
- UPS and FedEx freight for large or heavy items costs about four times more than other freight companies. Use them only for very small items. [L0855.062]
- For large and heavy items, UPS and FedEx freight rates are usually far above traditional freight shipping rates. Get quotes from traditional freight shippers before you set shipping prices. [L0972.010]
- Parcel carriers such as UPS, FedEx and USPS are usually expensive for heavy or bulky items. Use a freight broker or freight company for those shipments and compare quotes. [L0468.041] (older, 2020)

#### Source conflict: the same carriers, different price advice

- Brokers are about 50% cheaper than UPS or FedEx for large and heavy items. [L0251.010] (2023)
- UPS or FedEx freight for large or heavy items costs about four times more than other freight companies. [L0855.062] (2023)
- UPS or FedEx rates for small parcels are usually the best. For large items, their freight rates are often well above what brokers quote. [L0835.033] (2023)
- Use freight brokers for large or heavy items. Use UPS or FedEx for small parcels. [L0835.033, L0855.062, L0251.010]

### Find carriers through top retailers

#### Ask suppliers for their carriers

- Ask each supplier which carrier its largest retailers use for shipments of the same size and weight. Those retailers usually have the best rates. [L0854.008, L0835.029]
- Ask a supplier which freight companies its top retailers use, then request quotes from those companies, since they usually offer the best rate for that product size and weight. [L0972.011]
- Ask suppliers which companies their other retailers use, since this gives insider information on what works. [L1086.038]
- Ask the supplier which carriers its biggest retailers use. Get quotes from those carriers directly. Compare with freight quotes from FreightCenter.com, which is commonly used for LTL and white-glove freight. [L0803.021] (older, 2022)
- Use freight brokers such as FreightCenter.com for freight quotes rather than contacting carriers directly. The best carrier depends on origin and destination, so a broker usually finds the best prices. [L0803.020] (older, 2022)
- Asking a supplier's top retailers for their freight company, then contacting that company directly, can save around 100 to 200 USD per shipment. [L0966.038]

Procedure: finding a freight shipper for a product (2025)
1. Email your supplier contact and ask which companies their top retailers use to ship.
2. Ask for the names of those shipping companies.
3. Request quotes from those companies, since they usually offer the best rate for that product size and weight.

[L0972.011]

Procedure: getting a freight quote from the carriers your supplier's top retailers use (2023)
1. Ask the supplier's contact which freight companies their largest retailers use for shipments of the same size and weight.
2. Get instant quotes from several freight companies, for example at freightcenter.com.
3. Email the freight companies asking whether any discounts are available on the quotes.
4. Choose a carrier and pay it. Choose pickup at the supplier or warehouse when that applies.
5. Receive the bill of lading (BOL), which works as the freight shipping label.
6. Send the BOL to the supplier. The freight company picks up the order and arranges with the warehouse, and tracking information goes to the customer.
7. Start the shipment as soon as possible, since the BOL and pickup are arranged quickly.

[L0835.029]

Procedure: negotiating with brokers and your supplier's benchmark (2025)
1. Collect shipping quotes for the same shipment from freight broker websites such as freightcenter.com and Red Hawk.
2. If one broker quotes a lower price, send that quote to the other broker and ask them to beat it.
3. Use your supplier's quote for a shipment as a benchmark when negotiating with brokers.
4. Ask your suppliers which freight companies their top retailers use, then contact those companies directly for better rates.

[L0966.036]

Procedure: getting freight quotes from a supplier's top retailers (2020)
1. Ask each supplier which freight company it prefers and whether it can introduce you. Different suppliers have different freight relationships, and this often gets the best price.
2. If that fails, get quotes from a freight broker such as freightcenter.com.
3. Compare the quotes. Different quotes for the same shipment often differ by $100 to $300.

[L0852.043]

Example for carrier search (2025): ask the supplier which top retailers ship the item. Give the carrier the item's exact size and weight, for example 57 x 3 x 26 inches and 11 pounds. [L0857.031]

- Older (2023): Ask each supplier which freight company it prefers before you assume a broker's quote is the best. [L0852.043]
- Older (2022): A supplier that will not ship on its own account can be served by the carrier its top retailers use, as long as that carrier will pick up at the supplier's warehouse. [L0854.008]

#### White-glove carriers through top retailers

- For white-glove delivery, ask suppliers which white glove company they use or recommend for their product line, rather than finding a random local service. Suppliers already know which providers deliver their products well. [L0249.019] (older, 2022)
- For large and heavy items, suppliers recommend white glove partners. Contact them to learn their established process and which delivery companies other retailers use. [L0245.011]
- Ask suppliers which delivery companies they recommend. White glove pricing varies widely, so compare several providers and choose the one with the best price and reviews. [L0664.019] (older, 2020)
- For white glove with assembly, ask the supplier which top retailers it uses for that service. Those retailers likely already know the products and have set pricing. [L0970.042]

### Book the shipment and the bill of lading

#### What the BOL is

- A bill of lading (BOL) is the freight equivalent of a parcel shipping label. The freight company provides it, and you send it to the supplier so the freight company can pick up the order. [L0835.031]
- BOL stands for Bill of Lading. It shows the pickup location (supplier warehouse) and the delivery location (customer address) for freight shipments. [L0255.014]
- Freight transit estimates, such as four to six business days, are not a fixed promise until a delivery window is confirmed, so delays can happen before that point. [L0470.097]

#### Procedure: booking with a broker and the BOL

Procedure: arranging a bill of lading shipment via FreightCenter (2021)
1. Open the calculate freight rates tool on FreightCenter.
2. Enter the supplier's address or ZIP code and the customer's ZIP code.
3. Enter the package size and weight.
4. Compare the quotes from freight carriers and book the one you choose.
5. Download the bill of lading and send it to the supplier, whose truck then picks up the shipment.

[L0469.023]

Procedure: shipping through a freight broker, with the BOL sent to the supplier (older, 2018)
1. Place your order with the supplier.
2. Wait for the supplier to confirm that the item is ready for pickup.
3. Obtain a bill of lading (BOL) from a freight broker, a freight shipper, UPS, or FedEx.
4. Send the BOL to the supplier so the item is prepared for pickup.
5. The shipping company picks up the item from the supplier and delivers it to your customer.

[L0466.035]

#### Freight shipment for a cost-conscious owner

- Ask the supplier which freight company it prefers and get quotes from a broker, then book the cheaper option. Pay attention to delivery windows, since large heavy items are not dropped at the door. [L0852.043, L0805.022]
- For large freight, start the shipment as soon as possible, since the BOL and pickup are arranged quickly. [L0835.029]

#### Procedure for a supplier warehouse pickup

- Get the BOL from the broker. [L0805.023]
- Send it to the supplier. [L0805.023]
- Let the supplier arrange pickup. [L0805.023]

### Delivery levels: curbside, liftgate, white glove

#### Curbside and freight defaults

- Freight shipments should include free curbside delivery by default, with white glove service offered as a paid upgrade. [L0834.029] (older, 2021)
- Standard freight delivery for heavy, high-ticket items is curbside delivery, where the carrier leaves the box outside the door. White glove upgrades this so the crew brings the item inside, unpacks it and removes the packaging. It can be sold as a paid upsell to buyers of items costing thousands of dollars. [L0923.055]
- Large items shipped curbside: a PDF explaining the delivery process on each product page is sufficient at the start. Later, ask suppliers whether they offer white glove delivery and consider a markup for it. [L0856.056] (2024)
- Freight options include lift gate, inside delivery and white glove delivery, and each can be charged as a premium. [L0920.022]

#### Table of delivery levels (2020)

| Level | What the carrier does |
|---|---|
| Curbside | Default freight delivery. The product is left at the curb. |
| Lift gate | The truck lowers the product with a lift gate. The product is still not brought inside or to the door. Choose it over curbside when prices are comparable. |
| White glove | The freight carrier delivers to a local white glove company, which brings the product inside to the room the customer chooses, unboxes it, removes packaging debris and can assemble it. |

[L0961.033]

- Older (2021): White glove delivery comes in levels: delivery into the home, unboxing and removing packaging, or assembly and carrying up stairs with packaging removed. Ask each supplier which delivery and assembly partners its top retailers use for the product type. [L0469.025]
- Two tiers of white glove delivery: the Standard tier (free or freight shipping) delivers to the curbside, and the customer is responsible for unboxing and setup. The Premium tier (at upcharge) includes professional installation, unboxing, assembly and removal of all packaging. [L0245.012]
- White glove delivery means the product is brought inside the customer's home and can be installed or set up. It is the usual meaning of installation services in a niche. [L0856.057]

#### Levels of service for the freight carrier

- Older (2018): Freight delivery levels: the product is delivered by a lift gate when the customer's site has no loading dock. [L0471.056]
- White glove delivery typically costs 100 to 300 USD extra, depending on the product and whether assembly is included. Confirm the price before you offer it. [L0961.034] (older, 2020)

### Liftgate, residential and terminal fees

#### Definitions

- A liftgate is a hydraulic platform on a truck that lowers heavy goods to ground level. Residential delivery is a fee for delivering to a home rather than a business. A freight terminal delivery sends goods to a freight hub instead of the customer's door. [L0471.056]
- Liftgate is a hydraulic platform on a truck that lowers heavy goods to ground level. [L0471.056]

#### Include liftgate or charge it as an option

- Include liftgate services by default on heavy freight shipments to prevent unexpected fees. If a customer's location triggers additional charges (residential fees, overhanging issues), send an invoice for those location-specific costs. [L0276.027]
- Decide on lift gate and white glove charges by checking the top two or three competitors in the niche. Some competitors offer free curbside delivery, some include a free lift gate, and some charge extra for it. [L0468.043] (older, 2020)
- When booking a freight shipment, select the lift gate or white glove option through the checkbox in the freight booking. If the shipment is on the supplier's account, ask the supplier to add the option to the shipment. [L0468.042] (older, 2020)
- A single freight carrier quoted $200 to $250 for liftgate service on one shipment. A flat liftgate fee on every order can therefore be costly, so offer it as an option rather than a default charge. [L0667.016]

Procedure: adding liftgate as a paid option (2025)
1. Check whether competitors include liftgate service on their product or shipping pages.
2. When booking a freight shipment, add liftgate to the delivery if the carrier offers it.
3. If liftgate is not included by default, send the order confirmation first, saying the product is in stock and ready to ship.
4. Offer the liftgate upgrade and ask the customer to reply if they want it.
5. Get a carrier quote, and if the customer accepts, create a custom invoice in Shopify and send it.

[L0667.014]

Template for the liftgate upgrade offer (2025):

```
Thank you for your order. Your product is in stock and ready to ship. We also offer an upgrade to liftgate delivery service, which helps with delivery to locations without a loading dock. If you are interested, please reply to this email and let us know, and we will send you a quote for the upgrade.
```

[L0667.015]

### White glove and installation partners

#### Ask the supplier first

- Ask the supplier directly whether it offers white glove delivery and at what price. If not, ask which delivery company the supplier's largest retailers use. Work with the same companies, since they will give the best rates. [L0964.032] (2023)
- Ask a supplier to organize white glove delivery or installation for your customers instead of building your own service. Get the supplier's preferred provider and pricing, then offer it as an add-on. If the supplier declines, contact the top retailers' providers directly. [L0934.152] (2023)
- Products that need assembly or adjustment on arrival can work if the supplier already has a delivery and setup process. Verify who delivers, assembles and adjusts the product by asking future competitors which companies they use. [L0962.022] (older, 2021)
- For suppliers that already work with white glove partners: white glove is the carrier-based service the supplier's partners perform. [L0245.011]

Procedure: offering white glove delivery through a supplier (2024)
1. Ask the supplier whether white glove service is available through their shipping carrier, not through the supplier itself.
2. Ask what the upcharge is for it.
3. If the supplier says no, ask which other top retailers they work with that offer white glove shipping and which providers those retailers use.

[L0666.026]

Procedure: setting up white glove delivery (undated)
1. Ask the supplier for each brand which retailers they currently use for white glove.
2. Use those same carriers, since they already pick up from that warehouse and have pricing.
3. Set the price per product based on carrier cost and margin.

[L0941.028]

Procedure: arranging white glove delivery (delivery into the home, unboxing, assembly and removal of packaging) (older, 2019)
1. Ask each brand who it recommends for white glove delivery.
2. Ask for a contact name, phone number and email for the recommended company.
3. Contact those companies directly.

[L0357.018]

- Older (2020): Pilot is a white glove delivery company named as an alternative when FedEx Freight does not offer white glove service. Ask brands which white glove providers they recommend, because they may have deals that save you money. [L0961.035]
- Older (2020): Carriers should be chosen per supplier, based on the supplier's recommendation. [L0967.040]
- Older (2020): White glove delivery can be offered for high-priced items either as a paid add-on or as a bonus. [L0967.040]
- Older (2024): Do not provide installation or assembly yourself, because it adds the workload of another business. Outsource installation or assembly to other companies where possible. [L0666.027]

#### Pricing white glove

Sources differ on the price, and they describe different things:
- Typical carrier cost to the store: 100 to 300 USD extra, depending on the product and assembly. [L0961.034] (older, 2020)
- A heavy, high-ticket order: the store sold white glove for 500 to 600 dollars per order, while it cost the store around 200 to 300 dollars. The delivery service carries the item inside, unpacks it and removes the packaging, which customers buying items worth thousands of dollars often gladly pay for. [L0923.048]
- Price range set from the product margin and the average white glove cost for that product: 500 to 5,000+ dollars. It can be offered free when margins are high and the add-on is cheap. [L0941.027]
- Set white glove price per product based on carrier cost and margin. [L0941.028]
- White glove delivery pricing varies widely, so compare several providers. [L0664.019] (older, 2020)

#### Ask about white glove for the product

- Ask suppliers which delivery companies they recommend. Choose the one with the best price and reviews. [L0664.019] (older, 2020)
- Ask competitors who installs their product rather than building your own network of installers in different cities. Installation is often handled by a few nationwide companies that already work with the brands. [L0972.009] (2025)
- Explain shipping methods: the freight carrier delivers to a local white glove company, which takes over inside the home. [L0961.033] (older, 2020)

### Assembly and installation

#### Which products need a partner

- Products that need site work or a trade installer, such as Solar Panels, Wind Turbines, Garage Doors, Well Pumps, Hot Tubs, Saunas, Above Ground Pools, Fireplaces, Wood & Pellet Stoves and Walk-in Refrigerators & Freezers, need an installation partner or local service network. Without one, the sale ends at delivery and the return and complaint risk rises. [L0546.017]
- For products that need assembly of four or more hours, no national service was found. Ask the supplier for a recommended local installer, since many large retailers subcontract installation to local companies. [L1090.032] (older, 2021)
- Assembly services are seldom offered by suppliers. When they are, present them as an upsell rather than an included feature. [L0934.154] (2023)

#### Procedure: assembly on large items

Procedure: offering assembly on large items (older, 2021; the same procedure appears in two units)
1. Check competitor listings on Google Shopping. If they include assembly, offer it too.
2. Ask the supplier which retailers it uses for white glove delivery.
3. Contact those companies and build a relationship with them.

[L0671.042, L0671.181]

Procedure: finding a competitor's assembly partner (older, 2021)
1. Check the competitor's website for a named assembly or service partner.
2. If none is named, contact the competitor from a personal email address (not your business email) and ask which company assembles the product.
3. Contact that assembly company directly to set up your own partnership.

[L0834.116]

#### Assembly as an upsell

- An upsell for assembly can appear at checkout as a pop-up asking whether the buyer wants one of the brand's specialists to set the product up at home. When the shopper says yes, the store sends a nearby professional, or sources one through Craigslist, and keeps the markup. [L0923.049]
- Assembly as an upsell can be handled by hiring a local contractor per order, which means manual coordination. Alternatively, use a nationwide white-glove service that delivers and assembles in one step. The second option keeps the margin without extra coordination. [L0970.043] (2023)
- Do not buy from a supplier and hire local installers on your own unless no supplier process exists. Doing it yourself adds work and risk. [L0962.023] (older, 2021)

### Geo-targeting heavy products in ads

#### The principle

- For heavy items that are expensive to ship over long distances, sell nationally but run paid ads only in the area near the supplier's warehouse. Geo-targeting keeps shipping costs low for paid orders. [L0853.059] (older, 2021)
- Products from a heavy, large supplier may be sold with a geo-targeted campaign around the warehouse, so that orders ship only from a short distance and shipping cost stays low. For most campaigns, geo-targeting should be off. [L0964.045]
- To keep expensive heavy items profitable in ads, split suppliers into separate campaigns with geo-targeting. Put the heavy, low-margin supplier products into their own campaigns targeted within a 50-mile radius of the warehouse, so shipping costs stay low. [L0941.026]

#### Radius

- Source conflict: a 50-mile radius around the warehouse (L0941.026), against a radius of about 100 to 200 miles around the warehouse, with the 100-mile radius given as the example (L0964.046). [L0941.026, L0964.046]

#### Campaign split in Google Ads

Setting: campaign split for an expensive-to-ship supplier in Google Ads (2023)
- Make Alpha 1 and Beta 1 exclude that supplier's products.
- Create Alpha 2 and Beta 2 that contain only that supplier's products.
- Apply geo-targeting to Alpha 2 and Beta 2 around the supplier's warehouse.

[L0964.047]

#### Shipping-driven example

- Example of shipping-driven pricing: a 430-dollar shipping cost on an order that earns only 90 dollars of margin makes the item unprofitable. The fix is to split the campaigns so only that supplier's products run in a geo-targeted campaign around its warehouse. [L0964.044]

### Supplier location and reach

- For suppliers that ship to customers only in a limited city or region, look for a freight or logistics partner that can offer nationwide shipping instead of limiting the store to specific cities. [L0665.017] (older, 2022)
- Large or heavy furniture and similar bulky products from Europe are generally not shipped one by one to the US, because freight costs exceed the price. They are typically bulk-shipped to US fulfillment centers. Contact European brands directly and ask whether they have US fulfillment options. [L0665.024] (older, 2022)
- Heavy and large products such as furniture are usually not practical to dropship from China, and standard furniture delivery can take about three months. Sellers who want large items should look for supply through local platforms that offer China-sourced large-item fulfillment. [L0780.039]
- Freight-shipped items may need a freight broker in destination countries such as Canada. Ask the supplier which freight broker it uses for that country. If shipping is not cost effective, sell only in the country you can ship to profitably. [L0801.040] (older, 2021)

### Price shipping for the customer

- Add markups to shipping options such as expedited shipping and freight services. Each freight option can be charged as a premium. [L0920.022]
- Heavy items cannot be sold at a low price with free shipping, if the nationwide shipping cost is 100 to 150 dollars (up to 200 to 250 dollars for huge items). [L0665.067] (older, 2022)
- Shipping cost must be included when comparing a cheap product with an expensive one. [L0474.003]
- Set the selling price so a free-shipping offer still leaves the margin after the average freight cost. [L0801.039]

### Transit, delays, damage and returns

#### Delays

- Freight transit estimates, such as four to six business days, are not a fixed promise until a delivery window is confirmed, so delays can happen before that point. Compensation for a delay depends on the carrier's contract, so ask for it in writing, though carrier terms often limit the carrier's liability. [L0470.097]
- Freight carriers contact the customer to schedule delivery. Pass the tracking number to the customer as soon as you have it. [L0805.022]

#### Damage and packaging

Procedure: reducing damage risk for large items (older, 2018)
1. Ask which carrier the supplier prefers, or which carrier other retailers use.
2. Ask what their damage rate is, so you know what insurance to plan for.
3. Ask how they package large items. For experienced shippers, this includes foam inside the box, plywood around the item, and strapping to a pallet.
4. Check online reviews of the supplier for patterns of damage.
5. Mark these products as not eligible for return on your site, since they cannot be returned easily.

[L0466.037]

- Example: a 3,000 dollar marble dining table is shipped safely only with foam around it inside a box, plywood nailed around it, and strapping to a pallet. [L0466.038] (older, 2018)
- Ask suppliers for their packaging practice for large items, because damage risk depends on how the item is packed. [L0466.037]

#### Returns

- Mark large or heavy products as not eligible for return on your site, since they cannot be returned easily. [L0466.037] (older, 2018)
- Accept the own-account risk only for large or heavy items when no other option exists. Do not write the supplier off immediately. [L0466.036] (older, 2018)

### Special cases

#### Temperature-controlled freight
