- Temperature-controlled products can be shipped with refrigerated freight trucks for ongoing smaller shipments rather than full truckloads. Costs must be quoted case by case through freight carriers that offer cold storage. [L1090.029] (older, 2021)

#### Standard freight versus own account, and the Shopify question

- The ideal setup for freight is that the supplier quotes the order, creates the shipment and the BOL, and charges shipping on its own carrier account. The store does not set up freight shipping in Shopify. [L0854.003] (older, 2022)
- A custom invoice in Shopify is used only when a customer accepts an add-on, for example liftgate, and the store sends it. [L0667.014]

#### Heavy and bulky items from abroad

- Large and heavy items shipped abroad need a freight broker or carrier at each leg. Check the carrier at each stage. [L0801.040] (older, 2021)

### Tool and vendor overview

| Tool or service | Use | Source |
|---|---|---|
| FreightCenter (freightcenter.com) | Instant LTL quotes without binding to a carrier; compare carriers and calculate costs | [L0251.011, L0272.001, L0276.015, L0854.004] |
| Freight Club | Same service as FreightCenter, with potentially better pricing | [L0272.001, L0834.018] |
| Blue Freight Forwarder | Another broker some members use | [L0941.030] |
| Red Hawk | Freight broker website for quote comparison | [L0966.036] |
| Pilot | White glove delivery company; named as an alternative when FedEx Freight does not offer white glove | [L0961.035] |
| Freight Shipping Estimates | Named resource in a 2020 coaching call; no usage described | [L0426.006] |

### Work summary for the operator

1. Get the box size, weight and supplier location before you list a heavy item. [L0854.023, L0666.024]
2. Estimate the average freight cost from several destinations. [L0801.039, L0276.015]
3. Set a price floor so the margin covers shipping. Do not list cheaper items in that category. [L0357.019]
4. Decide who books the freight: the supplier's account is best; otherwise book through a broker. [L0255.011, L0854.003, L0805.023]
5. Get quotes from several brokers and the supplier's own carriers. Compare and negotiate. [L0966.036, L0855.063, L0970.038]
6. Ask which carriers the supplier's top retailers use, and contact them directly. [L0972.011, L0966.038]
7. Decide delivery level: curbside by default; liftgate and white glove as options. [L0834.029, L0667.014, L0961.033]
8. For ads, geo-target heavy products near the warehouse. [L0941.026, L0964.045]

### Freight Basics for Large and Heavy Items

- Large and heavy items that do not fit normal parcel shipping are quoted as freight. Request a freight quote before setting the price of a bulky item. [L1105.011]
- A freight calculator estimates shipping costs when you compare supplier or shipping options. [L1096.004]
- Less-than-truckload (LTL) freight has no fixed price. In a 2018 source, quotes for the same shipment differed by around $200 between companies. A 2020 source (older) says the difference can be a hundred dollars or more. The gap varies per shipment, so compare quotes for each one, even when you offer free shipping to customers. [L1109.008, L1111.037]
- Overpaying for freight is a costly early mistake (2020, older). Always compare quotes before booking a shipment. [L1111.038]
- For large and heavy items, freight brokers quote the shipping. Brokers are intermediaries, not the carriers themselves. Early on, quotes from different brokers tend to be similar. A quote of about $400 for a sub-$200 item shows that the selling price must be high. Competing quotes will not fix the margin on low-priced heavy goods. [L1114.068]

### Shipping Terms and Carrier Accounts

- Confirm each supplier's shipping terms before you build LTL costs into product prices. Estimate costs for the suppliers that do not cover them, because who pays changes the cost. [L1097.045]
- Some suppliers require the retailer to have its own freight account on file. They ship on that account and add the freight cost to the invoice. These charges can get expensive, so compare them before committing. [L1094.001]
- Shipping on your own account lets you track shipments, follow up with the carrier and file claims for the customer. This is easier when the supplier is not the point of contact for the carrier. [L1094.003]
- Setting up your own carrier account, when a supplier will not ship on its own (2019, older):
  1. Ask first whether the supplier will ship on its own account, since that is simpler.
  2. Create a free UPS account at ups.com or a free FedEx account at fedex.com.
  3. Send the account number to the supplier, who enters it when creating shipping labels.
  4. Pay the supplier for the product, and receive the shipping bill directly from the carrier.

  [L1110.007]

### Estimating Freight Costs Before Launch

- Freightcenter's rate tool estimates LTL costs from the supplier warehouse ZIP code, the customer ZIP code, and the package size and weight. Use it to decide on free shipping before any orders come in. [L1092.036]
- Freightcenter also gives freight quotes for shipments to Canadian addresses, using postal codes. Canada-specific carriers recommended by other sellers can be compared against it. [L1094.004]
- Estimating LTL costs with Freightcenter (freightcenter.com):
  1. Find the warehouse location of the supplier.
  2. Open Freightcenter and choose LTL (less than truckload).
  3. Enter the supplier's ZIP code as the origin.
  4. Enter the ZIP code of a hypothetical customer, for example 90210.
  5. Enter an estimated package size and weight.
  6. Click 'View my rates' to get the quotes.

  [L1097.014]
- Estimating average freight cost across destinations (2021, older):
  1. Go to freightcenter.com and open the freight rate calculator.
  2. Enter the supplier's zip code as origin and a test destination zip code in another state.
  3. Enter the shipment details such as weight and dimensions.
  4. Repeat with several destinations, such as one per region, and average the rates for the shipping cost estimate.

  [L1279.027]
- Testing parcel rates before the first order (2021, older):
  1. Ask the supplier for the product size and weight of the packaged item.
  2. Create test shipments on UPS and FedEx to see what they would cost.
  3. Do this before the first order so no unexpectedly expensive shipment arrives.

  [L1279.028]

### Getting Quotes from Freight Brokers

- Gathering data for broker quotes (2020, older):
  1. Look up product dimensions and weight on competitor product pages or the supplier price sheet.
  2. If they are missing, ask the supplier for them.
  3. Enter the shipment details into freight broker quote sites such as freightquote.com or freightpros.com.
  4. Use the averages in margin calculations.

  [L1111.036]
- Quoting large or heavy orders (2020, older):
  1. Ask the supplier to ship on your own account if possible.
  2. If not, request quotes from at least three freight brokers for each large or heavy order.
  3. Compare the quotes, since prices for the same shipment can vary by a hundred dollars or more.
  4. Choose the lowest acceptable quote.

  [L1111.037]

### Finding Carriers Through Top Retailers

- Finding freight carriers for heavy products:
  1. Identify the top competing retailers that already sell the product at MAP and check whether they add a shipping charge or raise prices.
  2. Contact one of those competitors from a personal email, not a business email, as a prospective customer. Ask how the product ships and which carrier they use.
  3. Ask the supplier which top retailers it ships through for these products, and say that freight quotes received so far are too expensive.
  4. Request quotes from those carriers or retailers so you can compare them against your own freight broker quotes.

  [L1115.006]
- Template for the supplier message in step 3:

```
Hi [supplier contact name], we are interested in [product name] and want to confirm how it ships. We have requested freight quotes from several brokers, and the prices so far are too high for us to make it work. We have also seen that some other retailers sell these products at MAP. Could you tell us which top retailers you currently use for shipments on these products, so we can request quotes from them as well? Thank you, [your name]
```

  [L1115.096]
- For installation-heavy products, ask the supplier which installer it recommends. These are usually the same companies it ships with, so request quotes from them (2021, older). [L1279.036]

### Heavy Products and Margin

- For very heavy products, roughly 300 to 1,200 lb, freight can consume most of a margin around 10%. Check the margin after shipping before ads run. A single item near 1,200 lb needs its own freight quote, because its cost cannot be estimated from lighter products. [L1115.094]
- Before testing bulky or large products, check the supplier or CJ shipping cost, since larger packages raise shipping costs. The product only makes sense if the supplier price and shipping still leave a viable margin. [L1185.015]

### Shipping Cost at Checkout and on Product Pages

- For heavy items, such as 25 lb or more with freight class pricing, do not calculate shipping at checkout. Add a fixed average shipping cost on the product page instead, so every customer sees it up front (2020, older). [L1277.040]
- Average freight cost for large, heavy furniture-type products in the worked example is about $250 per order. With free shipping, this cost is paid from the store's margin. [L1179.007]

### Booking a Freight Shipment

- Booking a freight shipment through an online freight broker. The example uses Interparcel, an Australian broker, so check the equivalent for your country:
  1. Open the broker's website (example: www.interparcel.com.au).
  2. On the home page, request a fast quote by entering the package dimensions and weight, the pickup suburb (supplier) and the delivery suburb (customer).
  3. From the price list, choose the cheapest option whose name ends in 'ATL'. If no option ends in 'ATL', choose the cheapest option overall. Click 'Book Now'.
  4. Enter the collection address (supplier) and the delivery address (customer), then click next.
  5. Pick the pickup date. It must be the following business day, so a booking made on Wednesday is set for Thursday. You do not need to set a time. Click next.
  6. Once the pickup date and time are confirmed, click next again.
  7. Type a short description of the goods, such as 'lights', 'lamps' or 'chandelier'. Tick the box below it and enter the value of the items, which must match the customer's order price. Leave the Loss/Damage Warranty dropdown at its current setting. Click next.
  8. In the Payment section, select the 'Stored Debit/Credit Card' button. This bills the company account automatically.
  9. Leave the card dropdown on its default setting, which is the company card saved to the account. Tick the box, then click 'Pay Now'.
  10. On the confirmation screen, download the Shipping Labels and the Address Labels and save them to your computer.
  11. Send both labels to the supplier along with the payment confirmation for the order. Give the supplier the pickup time and the carrier.
  12. If you need to track the order for the customer, use the saved documents.

  [L1291.009]
- On the price list, choose the cheapest option whose name ends in 'ATL'. If no option has that ending, choose the cheapest option overall. [L1291.010, L1291.009]
- Freight pickup is booked for the next business day, so a booking made on Wednesday is set for Thursday. A pickup time is not needed. [L1291.011, L1291.009]
- Loss/Damage Warranty dropdown: leave it unchanged at its default value. When declaring the goods, enter the customer's order price, not your supplier cost. [L1291.012]
- After booking, message the supplier with this text, and send it together with the shipping and address labels and the payment confirmation:

```
This order will be picked up on [pickup day and date] by [carrier name].
```

  [L1291.014]

### Freight-Only Suppliers, Receivers and Installation

- Freight-only suppliers may invoice the cost of goods plus freight to a commercial receiver of your choice, not to a residence. The receiver must have a loading dock and inspect the product for freight damage on arrival. Installation is handled separately and is not included on the invoice. [L1094.005]
- For heavy or freight-only items, the usual setup is that the freight carrier collects the product directly from the supplier's warehouse and delivers it straight to the end customer. Whether installation is needed decides if a separate installer is required. [L1094.007]
- In most cases the supplier books the freight with a freight company, is present when it collects the goods, and loads the truck. In some cases you must book the shipping yourself, but the supplier still packs and loads the box (2021-22, older). [L1480.014]
- Handling heavy or installation-required products:
  1. Ask the supplier which top retailers they use for installation of that product.
  2. Find a nationwide installation company already familiar with the product.
  3. Contact that company directly for installation pricing.

  [L1094.006]

### White Glove Delivery

- White glove delivery suits large, heavy or assembly-heavy products that are hard for a customer to move in. The carrier brings the item inside, unpacks it, does light assembly if needed, and removes all boxes and packaging (2022, older). [L1281.001]
- To offer white glove delivery for products shipped by suppliers that do not offer it (2022, older):
  1. Ask the supplier which white glove delivery company other retailers use for their products.
  2. Contact that company and build a relationship, since it already knows the products and has quotes.
  3. If no partner is available, limit a free white glove promotion to the eligible brands and list those brands on a dedicated Shopify page.

  [L1092.039]
- To offer paid white glove at checkout (2022, older):
  1. Open the Shopify shipping settings.
  2. Keep the default flat-rate shipping option at $0 (free shipping).
  3. Add a second shipping rate for the white glove upgrade with its price.
  4. Customers then see the upgrade choice when they reach shipping options in the cart.

  [L1281.004]
- For a custom white glove quote (2022, older):
  1. Tell the customer on the store where to request a quote (email, phone or text).
  2. Give the default free shipping on the order.
  3. Send the custom white glove quote only if the customer chooses the upgrade.

  The method works for any niche and fits margin-sensitive products. [L1281.005]

### Regional Shipping Cost and Geo-Targeting

- Shipping cost varies with origin and destination. Shipping within the same state is cheaper than shipping across the country. Cost estimates for heavy items should use realistic average values, not a single fixed number. [L1180.006]
- For bulky products sold with free shipping, prefer a supplier located near the main customer region. In the example, shipping within one state cost $150 versus $300 across the country. That difference alone changed net profit from $300 to $450 per order. [L1180.016]

### Bulky Products: Packaging and Shipping Method

- Volumetric weight starts to matter above about 1 kg. Bulky items are charged for the space they occupy, so large or heavy products such as furniture can become very expensive and may need special shipping lines. [L1217.013]
- Large high-ticket products such as outdoor structures usually ship disassembled in boxes, not fully assembled. Before listing a bulky product, confirm the packed form and shipping method with the supplier (2021-22, older). [L1481.027]

#### Further details

Freight cost check for a new supplier:
1. Ask the supplier for a freight quote for the first order from the carrier they use.
2. Get a second quote on freightcenter.com using the product dimensions, the supplier's pickup location and the customer's delivery location.
3. Compare both quotes and choose the cheaper option, or negotiate with the supplier using the lower quote.
[L1094.002]

## Order Processing and Automation

### Why Order Processing Needs a System

- Every order placed on the website must be shipped, which makes order processing a daily recurring task and a prime candidate for automation or delegation. [L0592.005]
- Automating order placement with suppliers is worthwhile only after the manual process is understood. [L0967.038]
- Speed matters more than the time automation saves. The sooner a supplier receives an order, the sooner it ships. Instant submission removes the wait between order receipt and supplier notification, which gets products to customers faster and raises satisfaction. [L0283.007]
- Track the status of every order at every supplier. Suppliers ship at different speeds, orders can get lost in supplier queues, and placed orders can slip through unnoticed. Proactive monitoring catches these failures and enables customer follow-up and review requests. [L0236.004]
- Without a system that records when each order went to the supplier and whether it shipped, customers may contact you one or two weeks later asking where their order is, and you have no proof of what was sent. [L1407.004]
- Payment processors may release money late when fulfillment proof is missing. Ship and track orders quickly, and send tracking information to the payment processor automatically with each fulfillment. [L1512.040]
- Write your own process checklists for order handling and similar daily tasks. The right steps differ between businesses, and written steps prevent gaps. Add a new step whenever a problem shows that one is missing. [L0949.010]

### Daily Order Check in Shopify

#### Checking and processing new orders

1. Open the Orders option in the left main menu of the Shopify Admin. All orders are listed from newest to oldest.
2. To find a specific order, use the search bar at the top of the page. You can search by order number, customer name or ordered product.
3. Always reference the order by its Shopify order number.
4. Check the orders page three times per day: in the morning, around midday, and about one hour before the end of the business day. Constant checking is not needed.
5. Process the morning check first, because many orders arrive overnight.
6. Follow the standard order flow: place the supplier order, capture payment, then fulfill the items.

[L1119.002]

#### Check schedules in the sources

Sources give different check frequencies. Both are recorded below. The sources do not state which order volume each schedule suits.

| Source | Check schedule | Units |
|---|---|---|
| Source A order routine | Three checks: morning, midday, and about one hour before the end of the business day | L1119.002 |
| Source C fulfillment routine | Once each evening | L1173.025 |
| Source A status folders | Check the order status folders at the end of each day | L1000.066 |
| Delegated fulfillment routine | Check at the end of each day whether orders have been fulfilled | L1360.017 |

- Check the status folders at the end of each day to find stalled orders, such as one a supplier never confirmed. This catches problems before a customer calls to ask where the order is. [L1000.066]
- Check supplier fulfillment timing by sampling random orders regularly, since slow fulfillment damages the whole funnel. Sampling random orders and checking that the supplier ships on time, because delays affect performance and the whole funnel. [L1259.057, L1260.057]

### Manual Purchase Orders First

#### Process orders manually before you automate

- Process orders manually before automating. Only switch to automated forwarding after you have handled several orders by hand and understand each step. [L0804.032]
- For a new store, beginners should forward orders manually rather than rely on automation. Before forwarding, confirm the product is in stock, and check that the order is not high risk. Send the order during the supplier's business hours. Orders that arrive late at night can be sent when the supplier opens. [L0807.014]
- Default to one email per order. Send each order to a supplier in its own separate email rather than batching several orders together, so that any problem with one order stays isolated and easy to track. [L1279.026] *(Older advice, 2021.)*
- Batching is the exception. If a supplier wants a daily email with a list of orders, send that list every day instead. [L0933.015]
- Some suppliers take about a week to process a purchase order, even when you pay quickly. Paying immediately by ACH can help, but slow processing can still happen beyond your control. [L1472.032]
- Suppliers with slow processing can still be worth working with if they offer good products and good margins. Accept the slower process, but keep customers informed. [L1472.034]

#### Standard manual order process (the most common method)

Sending an order to a supplier by email:

1. Subject line: store name, "Purchase Order" or PO, and the Shopify order number.
2. Body: item name, quantity (usually one), SKU number if one exists.
3. Add a "ship to" line with the customer's address.
4. Ask the supplier to charge the card on file and to send tracking information when shipped.

Some suppliers instead give a dealer-website login to place orders. The method is confirmed during approval. [L0964.021]

- Automating supplier order emails uses the automation covered in module seven of the course. For suppliers that require a dealer website login, a team member places the order manually. [L0964.022]
- Read the supplier agreement, which states how orders are submitted. [L0966.032]
- If orders go by email, send them to the order address listed in the agreement. [L0966.032]
- Use a subject line made of your store domain plus the Shopify order number. [L0966.032]
- In the body, state the quantity and SKU, the product name, and the customer's full shipping address. [L0966.032]
- If the supplier has a dealer portal, log in with the username and password issued after authorization and place the order there. [L0966.032]
- If the agreement does not explain the process, ask your supplier rep how to submit orders. [L0966.032]
- Typical order fulfillment with a drop-ship supplier: (1) the customer places an order in your store; (2) you send the supplier a purchase order by email listing the SKU, quantity and customer shipping address; (3) the supplier charges you and ships the item; (4) the supplier sends tracking information to you. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L0962.018]
- Manual fulfillment for early orders: (1) receive the order notification; (2) go to the warehouse where the stock is kept; (3) pick and pack the item; (4) ship it to the customer. This method is time-consuming, so it becomes a bottleneck as volume grows. [L1439.005]
- Pack and ship the first orders personally to learn the correct packaging and shipping process before delegating. When volume grows, hire someone who is shown the process and works at the warehouse on a fixed schedule, for example three times a week. [L1431.009]

#### Email templates for purchase orders

Standard supplier order email (store-wide layout with dynamic fields):

```
Subject: PO [purchase order number]

Hi [supplier contact name],

We would like to place an order for item [supplier item number or SKU], quantity [quantity].

Ship to customer, using the details below:
Name: [customer full name]
Address: [customer full shipping address]
Phone: [customer phone number]

Please charge our card on file and send the tracking information as soon as it is available.

Thank you,
[your name]
[your store name]
```

[L0360.009] *(2024.)*

```
Subject: Purchase Order [PO number] - [Store name]

Hi [Supplier contact name],

Please process the order below and charge the card we have on file.

Items:
[Quantity] x [Supplier SKU] - [Product name, size, color]

Ship to:
[Customer full name]
[Street address]
[City, State, ZIP]
[Customer phone]

Thank you,
[Your name]
[Store name] | [Contact email]
```

[L0804.031] *(2023.)*

```
Subject: [your store domain] order [Shopify order number]

Body:
This order is for [quantity] unit(s) of SKU [SKU number], product name: [product name].
Ship to:
[customer full name]
[customer street address]
[customer city, state, ZIP code]
[customer phone number, if the supplier requires it]
```

[L0966.033] *(2025.)*

```
Subject: [Store name] PO [Shopify order number]

Hello,

Please process the order below and send the tracking number once it has shipped.

Quantity: [quantity]
SKU or item number: [SKU]
Item: [product name]
Ship to: [customer full name], [street address], [city, state or province, postal code], [country], [phone number]

Thank you,
[Your name]
[Store name]
```

[L1279.025] *(Older advice, 2021.)*

Place a supplier order by email using this layout and replace every bracketed field with real data. The sender is the company's business address. The recipient is the supplier's address from the supplier details spreadsheet. The subject combines the company name, the word "order", and the Shopify order number. Open with a polite greeting such as Good Morning or Good Afternoon, then ask for the items: one line per product, showing quantity, product name and supplier SKU, with a line repeated for each product in the order. Under a "please ship to" heading, paste the customer's shipping details exactly as they appear on the Shopify order page. Close by asking the supplier to confirm stock and send an invoice for payment, end with a courteous sign-off, and add the full signature block.

[L1119.004]

- Every order email must use the same format and be sent from the company's business email address. Keep contact names, email addresses, phone numbers and websites for each supplier in a separate supplier details spreadsheet. [L1119.003]

```
Order {order number}: please ship this order directly to our customer {customer name and shipping address} and charge the card we have on file for this order. Reply to confirm you have received and processed the order, and send the tracking number as soon as it ships.
```

[L1407.024]

```
How do you prefer us to process orders? [If they want a daily email: That sounds great, we will send you a list of orders every day.] [If they have a dealer portal: That sounds great, we will place our orders through your dealer portal.]
```

[L0933.017]

#### Pre-written forwarding scripts

- Each supplier may require a different ordering method. Keep a pre-written order-forwarding script for each supplier in a shared sheet, so the assistant can forward orders quickly without searching for the procedure. [L1000.049]
- Example: a main store used around 35 different suppliers, each with its own ordering procedure. Keeping all of their scripts in one sheet made it possible to place orders quickly. [L1000.050]

### Supplier Ordering Channel and Agreement

- The authorized-retailer agreement from a supplier should state how orders are placed: through a dealer portal or by email purchase order. If the agreement does not say, ask the supplier how to submit orders after the first sale. [L0360.008]
- Let the supplier choose the order process, then confirm it. Keeping the process as simple as possible removes friction, which reduces the chance that the supplier hesitates to approve your account. [L0933.016]
- Ask the supplier how they prefer you to process orders. [L0933.015]
- If they want a daily email with the list of orders, send that list every day. [L0933.015]
- If they provide a dealer portal login, place your orders on their website through that portal. [L0933.015]
- Confirm to the supplier that you will work the way they prefer. [L0933.015]
- Route new orders based on how each supplier accepts them. Suppliers that take uniform emailed purchase orders can be handled by an order-sending tool. Suppliers with members portals can be assigned to yourself, a team member, or a Trello board. [L0466.042]
- An order automation can send each new order to Trello as a card, so that you or a team member can make sure it is processed. [L0466.043] *(2018.)*
- Some suppliers receive orders by email (for example, a Gmail address) rather than through a portal. This is normal for older, owner-run brands and is not by itself a red flag. [L0805.027]
- Supplier orders can be placed by email, through the supplier's own ordering website, or by phone call, depending on what each supplier offers. Whichever channel is used, the supplier confirms the order, then invoices you, and you pay the invoice before the goods ship. [L1480.035] *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)*
- Each supplier's channel can be summarized as follows:

| Channel | When it applies | Units |
|---|---|---|
| Email purchase order | Supplier accepts email orders; automation via Zapier is possible only for these | L0283.002, L0964.021 |
| Supplier's online ordering form | Stated as an alternative to email in the supplier details process | L1119.003 |
| Dealer portal or dealer website login | Supplier issues login after authorization; order placed manually | L0964.021, L0964.022, L0966.032 |
| Phone call | Offered by some suppliers | L1480.035 |

### Supplier Payment and Shopify Payment Capture

#### Who pays the supplier and when

Sources describe several models. Each applies to a different supplier arrangement.

| Model | How it works | Units |
|---|---|---|
| Supplier charges card on file | You send a purchase order; the supplier charges your card on file and ships | L0804.030, L0964.021 |
| Supplier invoices, you pay before shipping | Supplier confirms the order, invoices you, and you pay before the goods ship *(2021-2022)* | L1480.035 |
| Prepay the supplier | Customer pays your store; you verify, ask the supplier to check availability, the supplier confirms, then you prepay *(2023)* | L0835.034 |
| Ship first, charge or bill later | Suppliers ship automatically and charge the company card or bill it later; others require payment confirmed before they ship | L1119.005 |

- After the order email is sent, suppliers usually confirm stock and send an invoice. Know which group each supplier belongs to before you start the process. [L1119.005]
- Each supplier may require payment to be confirmed before it ships. Check this before you capture payment. [L1119.005]

#### Prepayment procedure for supplier-invoiced orders (2023)

1. Customer pays on your store.
2. Verify the payment and the shipping details.
3. Email the purchase order to the supplier and ask them to check availability.
4. The supplier confirms availability.
5. Prepay the supplier for the product.
6. Create the shipment on your own shipping account. For UPS or FedEx, give the supplier your account number so they create the shipment under it. For freight, get quotes from a freight service, pay it, and send the BOL to the supplier.

[L0835.034]

#### When to capture payment

Capture timing differs by source, and both are recorded.

- Pre-authorize, verify stock, then capture. Shopify authorizes the customer's card at checkout without capturing. You check whether the item is in stock with the supplier. If it is in stock, you capture the payment, which charges the card and lets you process the order. [L0358.024] *(Older advice, 2020.)*
- Pre-authorize, verify stock, then capture, shipping after capture. The same steps are repeated: authorize without capturing, confirm stock with the supplier, capture the payment in the Shopify admin, then ship. [L0671.063] *(Older advice, 2021.)*
- Note the conflict on when the customer is charged. L0358.024 says capture is the charge. L0671.063 says the customer is charged when the order ships (step 4), after capture. Test your own setup so you know which event charges the card.
- Choose by stockout frequency. If stockouts are common with your suppliers, pre-authorize, verify stock, then capture. If stock is reliable, capture automatically. [L1090.035] *(Older advice, 2021.)*
- Stores can be set not to charge customers until the owner manually selects that option, which ensures all ordered products are in stock before payment is taken. [L0856.060]
- Customer payments through Shopify Payments reach the bank account in about two business days (48 hours). For early orders, you can place the supplier order once those funds arrive. Only the first order or two need this workaround. [L0970.045] *(2023.)*
- Set the Payment Status to "paid" in the Shopify trigger settings. This prevents orders from being sent to suppliers before payment is confirmed. Only send orders to suppliers once they are confirmed as paid. [L0283.006]
- Do not process and submit orders to suppliers before payment status is confirmed as "paid". Setting this to "pending" or "any" sends unpaid orders to suppliers, which wastes their resources and creates fulfillment problems. [L0283.012]

#### Shopify capture and fulfillment procedure

1. Once stock is verified, either through your own records or the supplier, press the blue "Capture Payment" button on the order page.
2. A pop-up opens. Press the blue button a second time to approve it. This transfers the customer's money to the company bank account or PayPal account.
3. Before capturing, check whether the order carries a risk warning. If the risk level is medium or high, send the order to the business owner for direction before taking payment.
4. When the supplier has acknowledged the order, or after you have paid the supplier in cases where payment was required, press the blue "Fulfil Items" button on the order page.
5. In the pop-up, verify that the items being shipped are selected and that the "send a confirmation to the customer" box is ticked.
6. Press the green "Fulfil Items" button.
7. Shopify emails the customer to say the order is being fulfilled and then archives the order. You need to do nothing further.

[L1119.006]

- Shopify order page buttons: "Capture Payment" is a blue button, and its confirmation popup has a second blue button. "Fulfil Items" is a blue button on the order page. Its popup has a "send a confirmation to the customer" tick box and a green "Fulfil Items" button. [L1119.007]
- Sequence for fraud-screened stores: wait for the fraud-screening tool's decision and act only on its result; confirm with the manufacturer that the product is in stock, using a stock spreadsheet or a phone call; once you know the order will ship, capture the customer's payment; send the order to the supplier by copying only the needed details from the Shopify order notification email, deleting the parts you do not need, and including the order number. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L0949.009]

### Zapier Automation for Email Orders

#### Rules before you build

- Automation works only for suppliers that accept orders by email. Suppliers that require website ordering cannot use this method. If you have 20 suppliers and 2 require website ordering, you can still automate the other 18 that accept email. [L0283.002]
- Manual order submission by email takes about 3 minutes per order. With 500 orders in the first year, automating this process saves roughly 25 hours per year. [L0283.001]
- Create one separate Zap for each supplier that accepts email orders. This ensures each order goes only to the correct supplier and prevents one order from being sent to all suppliers at once. [L0283.004]
- Do not use one Zap for all suppliers, and do not skip the vendor matching filter. Without vendor filtering, one customer order could be sent to all suppliers instead of only the one whose product was ordered, creating confusion and duplicate work. [L0283.013]
- Use the "Filter by Zapier" feature with the condition "Line item vendor text exactly matches [vendor name]". This ensures only orders containing products from a specific supplier are sent to that supplier. The vendor name must match exactly what is assigned to products in your Shopify store. [L0283.008]
- In Shopify, each product is assigned to a vendor in its product settings. The Zapier condition uses these vendor assignments to route orders to the correct supplier. Without vendors assigned to products in Shopify beforehand, the filter cannot identify which supplier should receive each order. [L0283.016]
- Forwarding orders to suppliers automatically is an advanced step. Set it up only after the store is profitable and receives several orders per day. A connector tool for this costs about $20 per month, so it should save meaningful time to justify the expense. [L0853.108] *(Older advice, 2021.)*
- Auto-fulfillment tools (AutoDS-type) send orders instantly, which makes cancellations and returns costly once the supplier has already shipped. Some operators prefer to review orders daily before sending them to the supplier, and to connect the supplier to Shopify directly. [L1315.010]
- Automation tools such as AutoDS are better added later, once the store has a high order volume and the main need is automating fulfillment. Starting with them too early adds cost without solving a real problem. [L1172.019]

#### Zap setup procedure

1. Log into zapier.com and create a new Zap.
2. Name the Zap (e.g., "Automated Order Processing Vendor [Supplier Name]").
3. Step 1 - Trigger: Select Shopify as the app.
4. Select "New Paid Order" as the trigger event.
5. Choose your Shopify store from the dropdown.
6. Set Payment Status to "paid".
7. Set Order Status to "any".
8. Set Fulfillment Status to "any".
9. Click "Done Editing".
10. Add Step 2: Click the plus sign to add a new step.
11. Click "Filter".
12. Select "Filter by Zapier" as the app.
13. Set the condition to "Line item vendor text exactly matches".
14. Enter the vendor name exactly as it appears in your Shopify store (e.g., "Source A Merch").
15. Add Step 3: Click the plus sign to add another step.
16. Select Gmail as the app.
17. Select "Send Email" as the action event.
18. In the "To" field, enter the supplier's email address.
19. In the "Bcc" field, enter your own email address to receive a copy of every order.
20. In the "From" dropdown, select the email account you want orders sent from.
21. In "From Name", enter a name that will appear in the supplier's inbox (e.g., "Source A Orders").
22. In the subject line, type "Source A order PO " then click the plus icon to insert a field.
23. Select Shopify "New Paid Order" and select "Order Name" to create a dynamic order reference.
24. In the email body, compose your message and use the plus icon to insert dynamic fields.
25. Insert fields for product name, quantity, SKU, customer name and address from Shopify.
26. Format the email body to include all order details required by your supplier.
27. Click "Continue".
28. Toggle the switch from "off by default" to "on" to activate the Zap.
29. Test by placing a test order in your Shopify store.
30. Check your BCC inbox to verify the email was sent correctly to the supplier.

[L0283.005]

Zapier email template for a supplier (dynamic fields in brackets):

```
To: [supplier's email address]
Bcc: [your email address]
From: [your email account]
From Name: [Your Business Name] Orders

Subject: Source A order PO [Order Name]

Body:

Please process the order for [Quantity] [Product Name].

SKU: [Line Item SKU]

Shipping to:
[Customer First Name] [Customer Last Name]
[Customer Address]
[Customer Address Line 2 - if applicable]
[Customer City], [Customer State] [Customer Zip]

Let me know if you have any questions.

Best regards,
[Your Name]
[Your Business]
```

[L0283.009]

- Always BCC yourself on every order email sent by automation. This lets you verify that orders are submitted correctly and monitor the automation's performance. Review the BCC emails after a few orders to confirm everything works. [L0283.010]
- Zapier forwards orders only. The complete workflow includes one manual step: after Zap 1 creates a card in the paid orders list, move it manually to "sent to supplier" once you place the actual purchase order. This list shows which paid orders have been communicated to suppliers and which still need supplier contact, which helps prevent orders from being forgotten in supplier inboxes. [L0292.013]
- The "delivered and pending review" status is not automated. Zapier handles only the paid and shipped transitions. You or your VA must monitor carrier tracking data and supplier notifications to see when orders reach customers. Once tracking confirms delivery, move the card from shipped orders to "delivered and pending review" to trigger the customer follow-up process. [L0292.015]

### Order Status Tracking

#### Status flow with Gmail labels

1. Order comes in and is vetted with the fraud-screening service ClearSale.
2. Order is forwarded to the supplier, which must approve it. The order sits in an "awaiting approval" label.
3. Once the supplier confirms stock, the order moves to "approved, awaiting shipment".
4. Send the shipping details to the supplier and move the order to the next stage until it ships.

[L1000.065]

#### Status stages across the workflow

| Stage | Where it is tracked | Automated? | Units |
|---|---|---|---|
| Paid | Paid orders list (card created by Zap 1) | Yes, by Zapier | L0292.013 |
| Sent to supplier | Moved manually after the purchase order is placed | No | L0292.013 |
| Shipped | Shipped orders list | Yes, by Zapier | L0292.015 |
| Delivered and pending review | Moved manually after tracking confirms delivery | No | L0292.015 |
| Awaiting approval / approved, awaiting shipment | Gmail labels | Labels set by the team during flow | L1000.065 |

- Monitoring catches failures that would otherwise lead to lost orders and missed follow-ups. [L0236.004]

### Confirming Shipment and Tracking in Shopify

#### Fulfillment with tracking details

Sources name the Shopify screens differently. The steps are the same: enter the carrier and tracking number, then mark the order fulfilled.

- Confirming shipment when the supplier ships:
  1. Wait for the supplier to confirm that the order has shipped, including the shipping service used and the tracking details.
  2. In the Fulfillment screen of the Shopify Admin, select the shipping service and enter the tracking number in the "Tracking Information" section.
  3. Mark the order as fulfilled, which tells the customer that the order has shipped.

  [L1291.005]

- Fulfilling an order in Shopify:
  1. Click "Fulfill item".
  2. Enter the tracking number.
  3. Enter the shipping carrier used by the supplier.
  4. Click "Send shipment details to customers".

  Once an item is marked fulfilled, its status changes from unfulfilled to fulfilled, and the customer receives an email with the tracking number. [L1302.008]

- The order page offers duplicate, cancel and archive actions, plus print and create shipping label options. Printing and shipping labels matter only if the store owner ships the items personally. In dropshipping the supplier ships, so these are not needed. [L1302.006]
- Sources differ on the customer email. L1302.008 says the customer receives an email with the tracking number. L1119.006 says Shopify emails the customer that the order is being fulfilled and then archives the order. Both happen in their respective flows, so check what your customer actually receives.
- If a shipping app is used on top of the standard Shopify fulfillment process, write its steps into the shipping SOP so staff can follow them, including how tracking details reach the customer. [L1291.028]
- Give the shipping and returns SOP a version number, starting at Version 1, and increase it each time the document is changed. This shows staff which version is current. [L1291.029]
- When you arrange shipping yourself, add this line to the standard order message to the supplier: "Please provide the dimensions and weight of the parcel(s) so we can organise delivery." [L1291.004]

### Delegating Order Fulfillment

#### Why and when to delegate

- Outsource order fulfillment early, because it is a low-skill but very time-consuming task. Check the fulfilled orders closely at first, then monitor them routinely once the process runs as planned. [L1358.016]
- Order fulfillment is one of the first tasks to outsource. It is time-consuming, and it is not a highly skilled task that anyone can learn from online tutorials. It is commonly the first task delegated in a dropshipping business. [L1360.016]
- A dedicated order processing hire should handle order management and supplier coordination, including placing supplier orders on time. Supplier and order work is a common place where a single person's workload first breaks down. [L1334.029] *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)*

#### Delegation procedure

1. Make sure everything is set up properly before handing over the work.
2. Give the virtual assistant or team member access to your payment providers.
3. Check at the end of each day whether orders have been fulfilled.
4. For bundles, have the team member create a specific SKU for the variant in CJ, for example when product A is sold at full price and the same variant of product B is added as a free gift.
5. Have them add the SKU to the store, so that incoming orders can be fulfilled.
6. Check closely in the beginning, then reduce oversight once the routine works as planned.

[L1360.017]

- Bundle example: product A is sold at full price and the same variant of product B is included as a gift. This requires a dedicated SKU for that variant at the supplier CJ. The team member creates this SKU, adds it to the store, and then fulfills the orders that come in. [L1360.018]
- Bundle setup step: for a bundle that gives product B free with product A, have the team create a specific SKU for that variant on the supplier platform, for example CJ. Add it to the store before orders come in so the bundle can be fulfilled. [L1358.017]

#### Own-stock fulfillment model

Use this procedure if you hold stock yourself rather than dropshipping. It is a separate model from supplier fulfillment.

1. Rent a warehouse on a monthly basis and store all stock there.
2. Keep a record of every incoming order.
3. Have a small team pack the orders on set days, here Saturday, Monday and Wednesday mornings for about one hour each.
4. Place the blanket inside the branded packaging provided by the box supplier.
5. Take the packed orders to the post office each day.
6. Mark each order as fulfilled in the order system.
7. Stop renting the warehouse once the stock is gone.

[L0492.023]

### Supplier-Specific Procedures

#### Suppliers that ship in bulk to their warehouse

If the supplier insists on bulk orders delivered to their warehouse:

1. Ask why they do not ship direct to customers. It is often just extra admin for them.
2. Offer to pay an extra percentage per order or a flat few dollars per order to cover their admin costs. Some suppliers charge such a fee themselves. You can drop it later once you are making them money.
3. Alternatively, propose shipping to a central freight forwarding location that you arrange, and ask whether that works for them.

[L0929.026] *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)*

#### Shipping responsibility per brand

- In a dropshipping setup where roughly 80% of brands ship directly from the manufacturer, about 20% of brands must be shipped by the store owner. Record each brand's shipping responsibility so staff know which orders need a manual shipping step. [L1396.001] *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)*
- Mark brands you ship yourself with a yellow label: open the brand card in Trello, go to Labels, choose Create a new label, pick yellow and add it to the card. Use the card comments to note the extra shipping steps for that brand. [L1396.002] *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)*
- Suppliers that take uniform emailed purchase orders can be handled by an order-sending tool. Suppliers with members portals go to yourself, a team member, or a Trello board. [L0466.042]

#### Supplier platforms

- Connect the store to a supplier platform such as CJ, HyperSKU or Best Dealer, depending on the product. Check open orders each evening. Place orders with the supplier using the payment method for the supplier account. Send shipping updates to customers. [L1173.025]
- Source D course setting: under Settings > Shipping and delivery, the Custom order fulfillment section lets you add an outside fulfillment service by entering its email address. Click "Add fulfillment service" to connect a supplier or fulfillment partner that ships orders for you. [L1167.038]

#### Free gifts and promotions

- Free-gift promotion that ships from a different source (2020):
  1. Set up the promotion (for example, spend over 1,000 USD and get a free rash guard).
  2. Send each qualifying customer's details to a Google Sheet or directly to the supplier using an automation.
  3. The gift ships separately from the main order.

  [L1277.042] *(Older advice, 2020.)*
- A free gift can be sold as a bundled SKU with the supplier. Ask the supplier to ship the product and gift together as one combined item (for example, CJ can create a unique SKU for two products). This avoids a gift app. It works only if there is no sizing conflict for the gift. [L1363.035]

#### Custom sizes and size conversion

- For custom-sized products, the store must capture each buyer's individual measurements, such as width and height, on the order so the supplier can produce the item. Product option apps can collect these values. [L0467.037] *(Older advice, 2019.)*
- For clothing and shoes, you can convert sizes on the back end. When a customer orders a size, the supplier ships a size that fits better, such as one size up (for example, US 9 ordered, US 9.5 or 10 shipped). The label will not match the order, but the item fits. This can be set up through instructions to a private supplier or inside the DSers or CJ Dropshipping apps. [L1128.014]

#### Personalized products

- Sending a customer personalization such as an engraved name to the supplier:
  1. Ask the fulfillment provider which line item properties they need on the order and obtain their exact field names from their API documentation.
  2. Define each property in Shopify so the customer's input is captured on the product page before checkout.
  3. Check that the property appears on the Shopify order.
  4. Have a developer map the property into the supplier's endpoint so the value is sent with the order.

  [L0709.001]

- Many suppliers provide only an API document rather than a ready-made integration. Connecting a custom field to the supplier's order usually needs a developer to read the endpoint and send the parameter correctly. [L0709.046]
- On personalized products, vague or incomplete customization instructions can lead to wrong prints or wording. Send the exact text and every required detail, and confirm them before the order goes into production. [L1108.043]
- Customization workflow for personalized products:
  1. Let the customer enter the personalization, such as a first name, on the product page.
  2. Hire a designer, for example on Fiverr, to create the design from the customer's information.
  3. Send the design to the customer for approval before printing.
  4. After approval, send the order to the fulfillment provider to print and ship the product.

  [L1172.016]

#### Suppliers where you arrange shipping

- A supplier that ships with your shipping account requires a prepayment and a shipment created on your account, as set out in the 2023 procedure. For UPS or FedEx, give the supplier your account number. For freight, pay the freight quote and send the BOL to the supplier. [L0835.034]
- Add the parcel dimensions and weight request to the standard order message to these suppliers. [L1291.004]

#### Suppliers that require sales or order checks before shipping

- Fraud screening: wait for the screening decision before acting, and place the order with the supplier only after payment capture (see the checklist above). [L0949.009]
- Medium or high risk: before capturing payment, send the order to the business owner for direction. [L1119.006]

#### Slow suppliers

- Some suppliers take about a week to process a purchase order even when you pay quickly. Pay by ACH to speed it up, but the delay can still happen. [L1472.032]
- Accept a slower process from a supplier with good products and margins, and keep customers informed. [L1472.034]

### Quality Monitoring

- Check supplier fulfillment timing by sampling random orders regularly, because slow fulfillment damages the whole funnel. Sample random orders and check that the supplier ships on time, since delays affect performance and the whole funnel. [L1259.057, L1260.057]
- Payment processors may release money late when fulfillment proof is missing, so ship and track quickly. [L1512.040]

## Inventory, Stock-Outs and Backorders

### Tracking top sellers

#### Scope: track only the products that matter

- Only track inventory for the top sellers. Most suppliers rarely go out of stock, so tracking every product live is not needed. [L0671.060, L0671.197]
- About 80% of sales typically come from about 20% of products, so focus inventory checks on those top sellers. [L0671.061, L0671.198]
- Example: out of 1,000 products, about 200 may sell consistently, and about 10% of those, roughly 20 products from suppliers with weak stock, need close inventory monitoring. [L0671.062, L0671.199]
- If stock runs low for a frequently sold item from a supplier with weak stock, update its product pages to show the low inventory. [L0671.064, L0671.201]
- Typically 20% or fewer of the products uploaded to a store sell consistently, and those are the ones that need close inventory monitoring. (older advice, 2019) [L1069.012]
- Track best sellers, which are typically about 20 percent of products and produce about 80 percent of sales. If they run low, update stock manually on the website, since automated inventory sync is not required for the whole catalog. (older advice, 2020) [L1088.017]
- Concentrate inventory monitoring on the top 20% of products, which produce about 80% of sales. Update product pages only when a top seller is sold out or about to sell out. (older advice, 2020) [L1111.031]
- Track inventory only for proven bestsellers where stock-outs have historically been a problem, not across the whole catalog. For those items, check supplier stock and update product pages accordingly. (older advice, 2026 source) [L0808.004]
- Inventory tracking: for most products, back-order status is updated manually. Only the top 20 percent of products by sales should be tracked more closely, with inventory updated when stock drops. (2024) [L0856.059]
- Track inventory only for the top 20 percent of products that drive about 80 percent of sales. When those best sellers run low, update the product page delivery note and adjust ad budgets. (2024) [L1285.021]
- Real-time inventory tracking is not practical for a dropship store. Monitor stock levels only for your best-selling products. (2025) [L0966.029]
- Real-time inventory tracking is not needed for most dropship stores, because suppliers rarely offer a uniform inventory feed. A supplier inventory tool is only worth looking into if certain suppliers keep running out of stock. (2023) [L1114.021]
- Inventory syncing with supplier systems is usually not worth it because it requires many workarounds. Instead, monitor inventory only for the top 20 percent of products that generate most of the sales, and do not sync the rest in real time. (2023) [L1283.010]
- Track inventory for your best sellers instead of trying to automate stock sync across dozens or a hundred suppliers, because full automation has rarely been worth the effort. (2021) [L0968.008]
- Full inventory sync with suppliers is not used in the source business. Check supplier stock manually about once a week only for brands from which you sell many SKUs. (2021) [L0962.020]
- There is no automated inventory sync from suppliers. Update product pages manually only for best-selling products, using the inventory sheets suppliers send, and mark them out of stock or back-ordered with a date. (2021) [L1279.023]
- Ask each supplier which products are its best sellers, and focus marketing on those products. (older advice, 2019) [L1069.017]
- Stores with 3,000 or more products often sell only 100 to 200 of them repeatedly. Focus inventory monitoring on products that sell consistently, and skip the rest, including items that have not sold yet. (2020) [L0358.023]
- Monitor inventory levels of your best-selling products closely, especially during supply disruptions. Remove a product from ads once its stock falls to roughly 10 units or less, and add it back once the stock is restocked. (2020) [L0358.025]

#### Procedure: manage a large catalog

1. Identify the products that get ad impressions, clicks and actual sales. (2021) [L0968.009]
2. Build a list of your best sellers, roughly the top 20 percent. (2021) [L0968.009]
3. Monitor stock for those products closely. (2021) [L0968.009]
4. When stock runs low, state it clearly on those product pages. (2021) [L0968.009]

#### Procedure: identify and monitor the top 20 percent

1. Identify the top 20 percent of products that generate about 80 percent of sales. (2020) [L0468.037]
2. Collect the inventory report from each supplier for those products. (2020) [L0468.037]
3. When a top product runs out or runs low, update the product page manually. (2020) [L0468.037]
4. Leave the rest of the catalog on the regular inventory cycle. (2020) [L0468.037]

#### What to watch when supply is disrupted

- During supply disruptions such as factory closures, products with low inventory or no spare stock may sell out for a period. Check stock on your best-selling products first. (2020) [L0961.025]
- Demand for home gym and workout equipment spiked during the 2020 COVID-19 lockdowns, and popular items sold out quickly. Factory backlogs from the Chinese New Year shutdown, plus port congestion, can cause best-selling products to run out of stock for weeks or months. (2020) [L0358.002]
- Running out of inventory means no sales and no income, so the seller must monitor stock levels closely. This matters even more when the order is large and the sales cycle is long. [L1153.050]
- Request a stock list from each supplier you sell heavily, showing inventory for every SKU, since many send these weekly anyway. Use the stock levels to decide how much to advertise each product. (2020) [L0961.024]

### Getting stock updates from suppliers

#### Find out how each supplier reports stock

- Ask suppliers how they share stock information, for example through a weekly stock email or a dealer portal login. (2021) [L0968.008]
- Ask each supplier how they communicate stock levels: a dealer portal you log into, a weekly inventory spreadsheet, or a shared Google Sheet that updates automatically. Each supplier works differently, so confirm this for every one. (2024) [L1285.020]
- Inventory tracking is not automated across brands because every supplier uses a different method, such as weekly spreadsheets, a dealer portal or email. Record each supplier's inventory check method in a shared sheet so staff can follow it. (2020) [L1111.030]
- Track how you check inventory with each supplier (daily update, weekly file, online portal, or email request) and document this in a list your team can access. Procedures vary by supplier; normalize them for consistency. (older advice, 2022) [L0272.003]
- Do not send your own spreadsheet of products to a supplier and ask them to fill it in; ask the supplier which inventory report they already produce. Some suppliers send weekly or biweekly inventory emails, some send monthly ones, and others offer a members area. (2020) [L0468.035]
- The inventory report should show units in stock, units on reorder and the date the reorder arrives. Ask whether the supplier can copy you on an existing report or create one for you. (2020) [L0468.036]
- Ask suppliers for stock availability by email, for example asking whether a given SKU is in stock. Each supplier explains its order process when you are approved. (2021) [L0962.019]
- Multi-supplier inventory apps require consistent, reliable data from every supplier. Spreadsheets sent weekly are workable, but supplier dealer portals that show inventory often cannot provide real-time inventory feeds. (2023) [L0835.042]

#### Procedure: ask for the right inventory feed

1. Ask whether the supplier has a dealer login showing real-time inventory. (2023) [L0964.028]
2. If not, ask whether they send a weekly stock spreadsheet. (2023) [L0964.028]
3. If they have no way to share it, ask whether they can send low-stock alerts. (2023) [L0964.028]
4. Adapt to what each supplier already has rather than sending a template. (2023) [L0964.028]

#### Procedure: keep availability accurate when suppliers send updates only on request

1. Ask the supplier whether it has an online dealer portal or weekly inventory report that shows stock levels. (2024) [L1115.010]
2. If the supplier provides a weekly spreadsheet, use it to decide which products to exclude from ads. (2024) [L1115.010]
3. Re-check stock levels before any new ad spend is allocated. (2024) [L1115.010]

#### Procedure: weekly reports for top sellers

1. Request weekly inventory reports from all suppliers in Google Sheets or Excel format. (2020) [L0268.012]
2. Include SKU numbers and quantity available. (2020) [L0268.012]
3. For best-selling products (top 20% of products), manually update product pages when inventory runs low or item backordered. (2020) [L0268.012]
4. Don't use real-time automated inventory tracking for all products; 80/20 rule applies. (2020) [L0268.012]

#### Email updates and check frequency

- Ask every supplier to add you to their inventory update email list. Keep this list in use so you know about stock changes before customers place orders. (2020) [L0852.032]
- Do not contact suppliers daily for inventory updates if it is not necessary; instead request a batch update at a set frequency (e.g., daily at noon, or weekly on Monday/Friday) to integrate into their normal schedule. (2020) [L0245.006]
- Supplier updates such as discounts, discontinued items and new SKUs usually arrive by email from your supplier contact, sent to all retailers. Some suppliers also provide a members area, so ask your contact how to be notified of changes. (2024) [L0965.028]
- Suppliers usually announce discontinuations and new price lists by email, so track supplier emails rather than checking all products each week. Check inventory only for best sellers from suppliers known for stock shortages. (older advice, 2022) [L1281.012]

#### Supplier stock timing and lead times

- A supplier's stock timing should come from real ETAs (for example a container on the water with a fixed warehouse arrival date) before accepting orders for backordered items. [L0359.035]
- When stock is short, supplier lead times can stretch to 6 to 12 months. Check current availability before you commit to building a store around a product. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0949.008]
- Before bulk ordering, track current stock and project sales so you know when the next order must be placed. Because the lead time is 20-30 days, the reorder date must be calculated ahead of time. [L1217.005]

#### Do not rely on the feed alone

- A supplier's system can show stock that does not exist, so do not rely on the inventory feed alone for an order. Verify stock directly with the supplier before you sell or promise delivery. (2026 source; see also case L1472.029) [L1472.030]

### Out-of-stock handling on product pages and in ads

#### Stock thresholds for ads: the sources compared

The sources set different thresholds. Each row shows when that rule applies. Use the threshold that matches the ETA you actually have.

| Source | ETA or situation | Ads | Product page |
|---|---|---|---|
| L0468.050 (2020) | No close restock date | Turn ads off | Not stated |
| L0468.050 (2020) | About 1-2 weeks | Keep running ads | Show expected ship date |
| L0666.028 (2024) | 2-3 week ETA | Keep running ads and selling | Note on back order with estimated ship date near Add to Cart |
| L0808.005 (2026) | About 2-3 weeks | Keep advertising and selling | Note showing the backorder and ETA (short ETAs only) |
| L0805.002 / L0805.003 (2024) | About 2-3 weeks from warehouse arrival | Keep running ads | Show expected ship date |
| L1111.032 (2020) | 2-3 weeks | Keep ads running | Show shipment ETA |
| L1111.032 (2020) | 8 weeks or more, or no ETA | Remove from ad campaigns | Not stated |
| L0835.035 (2023) | About 2 weeks | Keep selling | Show ETA on order page |
| L0835.035 (2023) | About 3 months or no ETA | Stop paid traffic | Keep page live with out-of-stock notice and list in-stock similar products |
| L0808.006 (2026) | No ETA, or 2 months or more | Stop advertising | Note that the product is out of stock until a given date (do not set inventory to zero) |
| L0965.034 (2024) | 3 weeks or more | Stop ads, do not remove product | Note with out-of-stock status and ETA |
| L1277.046 (2020) | Backorder longer than about 3 weeks | Pause ads | Not stated |
| L1277.046 (2020) | Backorder with no clear date | Delete from Google Shopping | Not stated |
| L1115.011 (2024) | Longer than about 2-3 weeks | Reduce ad spend until about 2 weeks before restock | Not stated |
| L1115.011 (2024) | Several months, or no ETA | No paid ad budget | Not stated |
| L1113.028 (2022) | ETA within 3-4 weeks | Keep selling when out of stock is unchecked | Clear out-of-stock note with ETA; email buyers that item is backordered |
| L1113.028 (2022) | Longer ETA | Set Google Shopping cost per click to zero | Set inventory to zero, and zero stock for all variants |
| L0666.029 (2024) | A couple of months | No paid traffic; lower cost per click to 1 cent | Keep product with back-order note and ETA date |
| L0834.021 (2021) | Long backorder | Stop ads | Restart a few weeks before next shipment arrives |

Where the sources disagree on the 2-3 week and longer ranges, the nearest-matching row applies. The 2026 advice (L0808.006) is the most recent instruction for setting inventory to zero, and it conflicts with the 2022 advice (L1113.028).

- A backorder of more than 2-3 weeks calls for sharply reduced ad spend on that product; the lead time itself is usually the same for every store selling the item because they ship from the same warehouse. (2024) [L0805.003]
- Stop advertising products that are backordered for long periods, and restart ads a few weeks before the next shipment arrives. (2021) [L0834.021]
- Reduce ad spend on a product when its supplier backorder is longer than about 2 to 3 weeks, and keep it reduced until it is about two weeks from being back in stock. Products with no ETA or a backorder of several months should receive no paid ad budget. (2024) [L1115.011]
- Turn off ads for a backordered product when there is no restock date that is reasonably close. If the backorder is about one or two weeks, keep running ads and show the expected ship date on the product page. (2020) [L0468.050]
- If an out-of-stock product has an ETA of about 2 to 3 weeks, keep advertising and selling it and add a product page note showing the backorder and the ETA. Do this only for items whose ETA is short. (2026) [L0808.005]
- If an out-of-stock product has no ETA or an ETA of two months or more, stop advertising it. Do not set its inventory to zero; instead, add a note on the product page saying it is out of stock until a given date. (2026) [L0808.006]
- When a product is out of stock with a restock ETA of 2 to 3 weeks, keep the ads running and show the shipment ETA on the product page. When the restock is 8 weeks or more away, or no ETA is known, remove the product from ad campaigns. (2020) [L1111.032]
- When a product is out of stock everywhere, pause paid ads for that product and add a backorder note with the date on each product page. Keep the product listed so the status is visible, and do not remove the supplier immediately. (2020) [L0852.035]
- Products with a long lead time mean ads stay off; see the procedures below. [L0965.034]
- Update a product page's lead time only when a popular product is back-ordered or out of stock for an extended period, such as three weeks. Do not update every ETA regularly. (2021) [L0469.035]

#### Procedure: long backorder (8-10 weeks) in a multi-campaign Google Ads setup

1. Do not stop all ads; only reduce the default cost per click for the affected products in the alpha/beta campaigns. (2024) [L0805.002]
2. Keep reducing ad spend on those products until they are about 2-3 weeks away from being back in stock, then restore it. (2024) [L0805.002]
3. Keep selling the backordered products; directly under the add-to-cart button, show a note that the product is out of stock with an estimated ship date. (2024) [L0805.002]
4. After a purchase of a backordered item, send an order confirmation email to the buyer asking them to confirm they saw the backorder note and accept the estimated ship date. (2024) [L0805.002]
5. If the item is only a few weeks from arriving at the supplier warehouse (e.g. 2-3 weeks), keep running ads and just display the expected ship date on the product page. (2024) [L0805.002]

#### Procedure: backorder of about 2 to 3 weeks

1. Keep the ads running and keep selling the product. (2024) [L0666.028]
2. On the product page near the Add to Cart button, add a text block stating that the product is on back order and giving the estimated shipping date. (2024) [L0666.028]
3. After the order comes in, manually email the customer to confirm the ETA for shipping. (2024) [L0666.028]

#### Procedure: backorder of a couple of months

1. Keep the product on the site with a back-order note showing the ETA date. (2024) [L0666.029]
2. Do not run paid traffic to it. (2024) [L0666.029]
3. In the campaigns, lower the cost per click to 1 cent so no real spend goes to it. (2024) [L0666.029]

#### Procedure: long backorder with a longer lead time, three weeks or more

1. Stop running ads for the product, but do not remove it from the store. (2024) [L0965.034]
2. Add a note on the product page stating that the product is currently out of stock and giving the ETA for shipment. (2024) [L0965.034]
3. If a customer still orders organically, email them after the order to confirm they accept the ETA. (2024) [L0965.034]
4. Restart ads about two weeks before the product is expected to be back in stock. (2024) [L0965.034]

#### Procedure: out-of-stock product with no ETA

1. Cut paid ad spend for the product page. (2024) [L1115.012]
2. Add a note next to the add-to-cart button saying the item is out of stock and showing the ETA if one is known. (2024) [L1115.012]
3. Keep the page live so free organic traffic can still arrive. (2024) [L1115.012]
4. Below the note, show a section with similar products that are in stock and ship within 24 hours, so organic visitors can still buy. (2024) [L1115.012]

#### Procedure: ETA of about two weeks, three months, or none

1. If the supplier gives a restock date about two weeks away, keep selling and show the ETA on the order page. (2023) [L0835.035]
2. If the product will be out of stock for about three months or has no ETA, stop running paid traffic to it. (2023) [L0835.035]
3. Keep the product page live with an out-of-stock notice, and list similar products that are in stock. (2023) [L0835.035]
4. When the product is almost back in stock, promote it again. (2023) [L0835.035]

#### Low-stock thresholds for top sellers

- Monitor inventory levels of your best-selling products closely, especially during supply disruptions. Remove a product from ads once its stock falls to roughly 10 units or less, and add it back once the stock is restocked. (2020) [L0358.025]
- When a fast-selling product runs low at the supplier, update its product page manually to a back-order status with the expected date and an option to place a deposit. Inventory updates from the supplier are not synced automatically and must be handled by hand. (older advice, 2019) [L1069.013]
- Back-in-stock notification apps can be used to let customers sign up for alerts when a product returns. (older advice, 2022) [L1113.029]
- Check stock on your best-selling products first, and remove them from ads when stock runs low. [L0961.025]

#### Product page display rules

- Do not display the stock count on the product page for a backorder of 8-10 weeks, because a visible number such as three in stock makes buyers assume it ships immediately. (2024) [L0805.001]
- Show the expected ship date on product pages for out-of-stock or backordered items, and link to similar in-stock products that ship within about 24 hours. Never display a normal shipping time on a product that is actually backordered for weeks. (2020) [L1277.047]
- Do not hide, unpublish or delete out-of-stock product pages that will return. Keep them live, state the back-order status and expected return date, and block ordering, because removing pages hurts SEO. (2021) [L1279.022]
- Update product pages only when a supplier reports a change, not on a fixed weekly or monthly schedule. (2026) [L0808.003]

#### Zero inventory and Google Merchant Center

- Marking products as sold out with zero inventory while customers can still buy them can lead Google to suspend a Merchant Center account. The store then cannot advertise its products until the problem is fixed. (2021) [L0853.004]
- Set inventory to zero only when the product is not orderable. The 2022 rule sets inventory to zero for longer ETAs and zeroes all variants (older advice, 2022) [L1113.028]. The 2026 rule keeps the product listed with an out-of-stock note and does not set inventory to zero (2026) [L0808.006]. Both can apply: the 2026 rule governs the note and ordering, and the 2022 rule governs the zero setting when the product cannot be ordered.

### Backorders and customer communication

#### Accepting backorders

- Offer back orders for out-of-stock products when a reasonable delivery window can be stated. Many customers will wait for an item they cannot get elsewhere. (older advice, 2021-22) [L1522.029]
- Under-promise and over-deliver on back orders: quote a longer lead time than the expected one. Example: if stock is expected in four weeks, promise six weeks, so early delivery earns better reviews and referrals. (older advice, 2021-22) [L1522.030]
- Backorders can be accepted, but the order confirmation should state that the product is backordered and give the expected ship date. This prevents complaints and refund requests later. (2020) [L1277.048]
- Only pre-sell a backordered product when the supplier has given a reliable ETA, because customers will cancel or complain if a long backorder is not communicated. (2021) [L0942.010]
- Fast-selling products that run low at the supplier can be listed as back-order with a deposit option (see L1069.013 above; older advice, 2019). [L1069.013]
- If a supplier is out of stock and gives no restock date, do not sell or advertise that supplier's products. If restock is coming soon in a meaningful quantity, pre-selling can make sense; decide supplier by supplier. (2021) [L0968.010]

#### Notice above Add to Cart when a supplier warehouse closes

- When a supplier warehouse closes, place a clear notice above the Add to Cart button stating due to the coronavirus, supplier ABC is currently not shipping until [estimated date] so customers make informed decisions before payment. (2020) [L0245.004]
- Warehouse closures in major hubs can delay shipping and require special arrangements with customers. (2020) [L0967.037]

#### Procedure: warehouse closure follow-up

1. Place warehouse closure notice above Add to Cart button. (2020) [L0245.005]
2. After orders arrive, manually follow up with phone calls and emails to each customer. (2020) [L0245.005]
3. Confirm customer willingness to wait for delayed shipment. (2020) [L0245.005]
4. Prevent customers from discovering shipping delays after payment. (2020) [L0245.005]

#### Procedure: handling a backordered product

1. Check the ETA for the backordered item with the supplier before speaking to the customer. (2020) [L0852.033]
2. Look for a similar product in your store, or find one from another supplier at a similar or lower price. A slightly more expensive alternative can also work if the extra cost is small. (2020) [L0852.033]
3. Contact the customer by phone, or have the phone team do it, and explain that the ETA applies to every seller of the product. (2020) [L0852.033]
4. If the customer will wait, offer to ship first out the door when stock arrives. If the wait is long, give the new ship date and make the same offer. (2020) [L0852.033]
5. If the customer needs the product sooner, offer the alternative. A cheaper alternative can come with a partial refund (for example $100) and ship within 24 to 48 hours. A slightly more expensive alternative can ship with no extra charge to the customer. (2020) [L0852.033]
6. If no similar product exists, look for similar products even from smaller or less established brands. (2020) [L0852.033]

#### Procedure: order check before taking payment

1. When an order arrives for a product that may be out of stock, check inventory with the supplier or warehouse before taking payment. (2020) [L1111.033]
2. If it is unavailable, email or call the customer that the card has not been charged yet. (2020) [L1111.033]
3. Tell the customer the backorder length. (2020) [L1111.033]
4. Let the customer choose to keep the order pending or cancel it, and release any pending charge. (2020) [L1111.033]

#### Procedure: out-of-stock product with no confirmed ETA, offer a similar item

1. Ask the supplier for an ETA and relay it to the customer if one exists. (2024) [L0965.030]
2. If there is no ETA, look through your own catalog for the closest similar product, matching type, size and color, for example a white standing desk about 80 inches long. (2024) [L0965.030]
3. Contact the customer and explain that the warehouse is out of the item with no confirmed return date. (2024) [L0965.030]
4. Offer the similar product, state the price difference in dollars, say whether it is in stock and can ship the next day. (2024) [L0965.030]
5. Ask whether the customer wants the order changed to the replacement product. (2024) [L0965.030]

Message to the customer (2024):

```
Hi [customer name],

Thank you for your order of [original product name]. Unfortunately, our supplier's warehouse is out of this item and they cannot confirm when it will be back in stock.

We found a very similar [product type, size, color] from another supplier that is in stock and can ship tomorrow. It costs [amount] dollars [more / less] than your original item.

Would you like us to change your order to this product? Please reply and let us know.

Thank you,
[your name / store name]
```
[L0965.031]

#### Procedure: pre-selling a product when a supplier gives an ETA

1. Use the supplier's ETA as the shipping date for the product. (2021) [L0942.009]
2. Turn the product's ads back on and let customers know it ships on that date. (2021) [L0942.009]
3. Show the shipping date in a clear but not overly loud notice right next to the Add to Cart button. (2021) [L0942.009]
4. Once orders come in, place them with the supplier so the goods are ready to ship when stock arrives. (2021) [L0942.009]

#### Procedure: backorder strategy with suppliers

1. Request inventory reports showing stock levels. (2023) [L0804.029]
2. Identify the top 20% of SKUs by sales. (2023) [L0804.029]
3. Find out when the next reorder arrives. (2023) [L0804.029]
4. When stock runs low, place a deposit to secure inventory before competitors run out. (2023) [L0804.029]

#### Refund and alternative email to a customer who asked for a refund

Use this when the updated ship date is known and the customer has asked for a refund. (2020)

```
Subject: Your order [order number] - updated shipping date

Hi [customer name],
```
